# Algebraic Kuga–Satake correspondences and Hodge conjectures on a K3 quadratic locus

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-M-0071` | `PM-032.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Algebraic-Kuga-Satake-correspondences-and-Hodge-conjectures-on-a-K3-quadratic-locus-September-30-2026` | date 2026-09-30 | derived from the openai/math preprint Algebraic-Kuga-Satake-correspondences-and-Hodge-conjectures-on-a-K3-quadratic-locus-September-30-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the rational Hodge and generalized Hodge conjectures in every cohomological degree of every self-power of a projective complex K3 surface whose transcendental quadratic space, with its cup-product form, admits a rational isometric embedding in $`\\mathbb U_{\\mathbb Q}^{\\oplus2}\\perp\\langle-1\\rangle^4`$. This includes every ample <i>P</i>-polarized K3 surface, including all Picard jumps, for $`P=\\mathbb U\\oplus D_8(-1)\\oplus D_4(-1)`$. On this locus we construct algebraic correspondences inducing every prescribed standard even-Clifford Kuga–Satake tensor. More generally, for any projective complex K3 surface, algebraicity of one exact standard even-Clifford Kuga–Satake tensor implies both conjectures for every self-power.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the rational Hodge and generalized Hodge conjectures in every cohomological degree of every self-power of a projective complex K3 surface whose transcendental quadratic space, with its cup-product form, admits a rational isometric embedding in $`\mathbb U_{\mathbb Q}^{\oplus2}\perp\langle-1\rangle^4`$. This includes every ample <i>P</i>-polarized K3 surface, including all Picard jumps, for $`P=\mathbb U\oplus D_8(-1)\oplus D_4(-1)`$. On this locus we construct algebraic correspondences inducing every prescribed standard even-Clifford Kuga–Satake tensor. More generally, for any projective complex K3 surface, algebraicity of one exact standard even-Clifford Kuga–Satake tensor implies both conjectures for every self-power.

- **claim-ext-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ext-criterion)): Let $S$ be a projective K3 surface. Suppose that, for one rational nonisotropic $w\in T_S$, one rational polarization, and one full even-Clifford realization, the tensor of $j_w(v)(a)=vaw$ is induced by a cycle in $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A^2)_{\mathbb Q}$. Then rational HC and rational GHC hold in every degree of every self-power of $S$.
  - Explanation: Algebraicity of one exact standard even-Clifford Kuga–Satake tensor on a projective K3 surface yields rational HC and GHC for every self-power.
- **claim-main-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): Let $S$ be a smooth projective complex K3 surface. Suppose that there is a rational isometric embedding $$(T_S,q)\hookrightarrow V_P.$$ For any full even-Clifford realization $H^1(A,\mathbb Q)=C^+(T_S,q)$, any rational nonisotropic $w\in T_S$, and any rational polarization $E$ of this weight-one Hodge structure, there is a cycle $$\Gamma_S\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A\times A)_{\mathbb Q}$$ whose action on every $x\in T_S$ is exactly $\iota_{w,E}(x)$ in $H^2(A\times A,\mathbb Q)$. The same assertion holds on a transported isogenous model with its specified tensor.* *Moreover, the rational Hodge conjecture and the rational generalized Hodge conjecture hold in every cohomological degree of every self-power $S^m$, for every integer $m\ge1$.
  - Explanation: If the transcendental quadratic space embeds rationally and isometrically in V_P, the prescribed tensor is induced by an algebraic cycle, and HC and GHC hold on all self-powers.
- **claim-p-lattice-complement** (classification, [source](#stmt-main-lattice)): There is a primitive embedding of $P$ in $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}=\mathbb U^{\oplus3}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus2}$ with orthogonal complement $T_P=\mathbb U\oplus\mathbb U(2)\oplus D_4(-1)$. For every primitive embedding of $P$ in the K3 lattice, the rational orthogonal complement is isometric to $V_P$.
  - Explanation: The lattice P embeds primitively in the K3 lattice, and every such embedding has rational orthogonal complement isometric to V_P.
- **claim-exterior-returns** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-conditional)): Let $S$ be a projective K3 surface with transcendental space $(T,q)$ of rational dimension $t$, put $V=T(1)$, and let $W=C^+(T,q)=H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$ be an even-Clifford Kuga–Satake realization. Suppose that for one rational nonisotropic $w\in T$ the tensor form of $$\label{ext:exact-kappa} j_w:V\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(W),\qquad j_w(v)(a)=vaw,$$ is induced by a cycle in $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A^2)_{\mathbb Q}$, using a rational polarization of $W$. Then, for every $1\leq k\leq t$, there is an injective algebraic Hodge map $$F_k:\bigwedge^kV\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(W)$$ with an algebraic left inverse on cohomology. More precisely, put $Z=A^2$ and $n=\dim Z$. There are correspondences $$\Gamma_k\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^{k+1}(S^k\times Z)_\mathbb Q,\qquad R_k\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^{n+k-1}(Z\times S^k)_\mathbb Q.$$ Viewing $\bigwedge^kV$ in $H^{2k}(S^k,\mathbb Q)(k)$ and identifying $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(W)\simeq(H^1(A,\mathbb Q)\otimes H^1(A,\mathbb Q))(1)\subset H^2(Z,\mathbb Q)(1)$ by a rational polarization, their weight-zero actions satisfy $(\Gamma_k)_*\|_{\wedge^kV}=F_k$ and $(R_k)_*F_k=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$. Every rational Hodge class in $\bigwedge^kV$ is the class of a rational algebraic cycle of codimension $k$ on $S^k$.
  - Explanation: From the single Clifford tensor cycle one gets injective algebraic Hodge maps from exterior powers into End(W) with algebraic left inverses, so Hodge classes in exterior powers are algebraic.
- **claim-cycle-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ghc-lem-cycle-support)): Let $X,Y$ be smooth projective varieties, with $\dim X=N$ and $\dim Y=D$, and let $C\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^{N+p}(X\times Y)_\mathbb Q$, where $p\ge0$. There is a fixed closed subset $B\subset Y$ of codimension at least $p$ supporting the image of $C_*$ on cohomology. In particular, $$C_*H^j(X,\mathbb Q)\subset N^pH^{j+2p}(Y,\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: A correspondence of the stated codimension has image supported in a closed subset of codimension at least p, hence lands in geometric coniveau p.
- **claim-exterior-coniveau** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ghc-prop-exterior-coniveau)): Let $S$ satisfy the exact even-Clifford algebraicity hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#ext:conditional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:conditional">4</a>, and put $t=\dim_\mathbb QT$. For every $1\le k\le t$, $$\bigwedge^kT\subset N^{k-1}H^{2k}(S^k,\mathbb Q).$$ Moreover every rational sub-Hodge structure of this exterior power satisfies the generalized Hodge conjecture in its ambient $H^{2k}(S^k,\mathbb Q)$. In particular, the top exterior line is supported in codimension $t$.
  - Explanation: Exterior powers of the transcendental space lie in coniveau at least k-1 and their sub-Hodge structures satisfy GHC in the ambient cohomology.
- **claim-orthogonal-carriers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ghc-lem-orthogonal-carriers)): Let $V=T(1)$ for a projective K3 surface with $\operatorname{Hg}(V)=\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(V,q)$ and $t=\dim V\ge3$. Let $H$ be a nontrivial rational simple representation occurring in a direct sum of tensor powers of $V$, endowed with its induced weight-zero Hodge structure. If its Hodge level is at most $2b$, there are integers $c,s\ge1$ and $k_1,\ldots,k_c\ge1$ such that $$c\le b,\qquad s=\sum_i k_i,\qquad k_i\le t, \qquad \bigotimes_{i=1}^c\bigwedge^{k_i}V\longrightarrow H$$ is a surjective rational Hodge map.
  - Explanation: In the special orthogonal case, a simple representation of bounded Hodge level is a quotient of a tensor product of few exterior powers.
- **claim-k3-carriers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ghc-lem-k3-carriers)): Let $V=T(1)$ for a projective K3 surface and let $G=\operatorname{Hg}(V)$. If a nontrivial rational simple representation $H$ occurring in a direct sum of tensor powers of $V$ has induced Hodge level at most $2b$, then there are $1\le c\le b$ and $1\le k_1,\ldots,k_c\le\dim_\mathbb QV$ with a surjective rational Hodge map $$\bigotimes_{j=1}^c\bigwedge^{k_j}V\longrightarrow H.$$
  - Explanation: The exterior-carrier bound is extended to every endomorphism-field case, real or CM.
- **claim-double-cover-structure** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-structure)): There is a smooth double cover $Y\to K=\mathop{\mathrm{Km}}(B')$, with $K$ the resolved Kummer surface, satisfying the following assertions.* 1. *The anti-invariant lattice $$V=H^2(Y,\mathbb Z)^{\delta=-1}$$ (with torsion removed) is even, has signature $(2,14)$, and has $2$-elementary discriminant group. For fixed $(B,M,\chi)$, monodromy acts trivially on this discriminant group.* 2. *There are eight mutually orthogonal algebraic anti-invariant classes $a_1,\ldots,a_8$, each of square $-4$. Their perpendicular $$U=\langle a_1,\ldots,a_8\rangle^\perp\subset V_\mathbb Q$$ is a Hodge structure of signature $(2,6)$ and, with the intersection form inherited from $Y$, is rationally isometric to $$V_P=(\mathbb U\oplus\mathbb U(2)\oplus D_4(-1))_\mathbb Q.$$* 3. *Resolved pullback gives a flat Hodge isomorphism $\beta:U\xrightarrow{\sim}U_X$ with $$(\beta x,\beta y)_X=2(x,y)_Y.$$ The algebraic projector $$\label{geom:projector} e=\frac14(\Delta_X+\Gamma_j-\Gamma_\delta-\Gamma_{j\delta}) \in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(X\times X)_\mathbb Q$$ has image $U_X$ in degree two and is zero in every other degree.* *The constructions are compatible with variation on the indicated smooth parameter opens.
  - Explanation: A smooth double cover of the resolved Kummer surface has an anti-invariant lattice whose rank-eight perpendicular is rationally isometric to V_P and is identified with an eigenspace in the double-cover surface by an algebraic projector.
- **claim-twisted-hodge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-twisted-hodge)): Put $E=T_B(-\log D)$. There is a flat complex identification of $U_X$ with a character eigenspace having Hodge components $$\label{geom:hodge-eigensheaves} U_X^{p,q}\simeq H^q\bigl(B,\Omega_B^p(\log D)\otimes M^{-1}\chi\bigr), \qquad p+q=2.$$ In particular, $U_X^{2,0}=H^0(B,M\chi)$ is one-dimensional. Fix a nonzero translation-invariant volume form $\Omega$ on $B$, and let $\theta\in H^0(B,M\chi)$ represent this line. Under <a href="#geom:hodge-eigensheaves" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geom:hodge-eigensheaves">[geom:hodge-eigensheaves]</a>, the period differential is the map on $H^1$ induced by $$\label{geom:derivative} E\xrightarrow{\ \theta\ }E\otimes M\chi \simeq\Omega_B^1(\log D)\otimes M^{-1}\chi.$$ The Kodaira–Spencer map identifies the tangent space of the full six-dimensional family $(B,M,\chi,[d])$ with $H^1(B,E)$.
  - Explanation: The eigenspace is identified with twisted logarithmic cohomology and the period differential is multiplication by a section; Kodaira–Spencer identifies the full parameter tangent space.
- **claim-period-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-period)): The period map of $U$, equivalently of $U_X$, has rank six on a nonempty Zariski-open subset of the six-dimensional parameter space. For a very general parameter, its rational Hodge structure is simple, has no nonzero rational $(1,1)$-class, and has Hodge group $\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(U)$.
  - Explanation: The period map has full rank on a Zariski open set; very general fibres are simple with no rational (1,1)-class and full special orthogonal Hodge group.
- **claim-gauss-generic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-gauss-generic)): For $(B,D)$ in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of the parameter space, $D$ has no nontrivial translation stabilizer. Its quotient $C=D/\{\pm1\}$ is a smooth genus-three curve, and the connected étale cover $D\to C$ is defined by a nontrivial line bundle $\epsilon\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[2]$. The Gauss map is the composite $$D\longrightarrow C\xrightarrow{\|K_C\otimes\epsilon\|}\mathbf P^1,$$ of degrees two and four. No fiber of the degree-four map has all its multiplicities even. The good open remains nonempty on every connected component obtained by adjoining the finite theta, torsion, and level choices used to define $X$.
  - Explanation: Generically the quotient curve has genus three, the Gauss map factors through a twisted canonical pencil, and no fibre is all-even.
- **claim-coherent-halves** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-halves)): There is an algebraic map $h:\mathcal S\to B$ such that $$\label{geom:coherent} 2h(s)=\sum_{p\in s}p,\qquad h(s[p])=h(s)-p.$$ The selections split globally into two degree-eight covers $\mathcal S_+\sqcup\mathcal S_-$ according to the parity of the number of sign changes. Their smooth projective completions $C_\pm$, which may be disconnected, carry extensions $a_\pm:C_\pm\to B$ of $h$. Wherever its differential is nonzero, either map is tangent to the direction recorded by its parameter in $\mathbf P^1$.
  - Explanation: Coherent half sums of selections exist algebraically and split into two parity covers whose maps are tangent to the Gauss direction.
- **claim-signed-relations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-relations)): On a regular-value disc, no signed sum of a nonempty subset of the four local branches $x_i$ is constant. Every connected component of $C_+$ and $C_-$ maps nonconstantly to $B$. No image component from $C_+$ is an opposite translate of an image component from $C_-$; that is, their image curves cannot satisfy $Z_+=b-Z_-$ for any $b\in B$.
  - Explanation: No signed partial sum of local branches is constant, and the two parity image curves are not opposite translates.
- **claim-curve-domination** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-domination)): For every parameter in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of the parameter space, there exist smooth projective connected curves $C_1,C_2$ and a dominant generically finite morphism $$g:C_1\times C_2\longrightarrow X.$$ Whenever additionally $U_X$ has no nonzero rational $(1,1)$-class, an algebraic correspondence induces an injection $$U_X\hookrightarrow H^1(C_1,\mathbb Q)\otimes H^1(C_2,\mathbb Q).$$ In particular, this injection exists at a very general parameter.
  - Explanation: The degree-eight addition map lifts to a dominant generically finite map from a product of curves onto the double cover, giving an algebraic injection into the tensor of curve cohomologies.
- **claim-ordered-genericity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-genericity)): Every nonempty regular period chart admits a smaller normalized product chart $A\times Q$ of abelian data and affine section coordinates as in Section <a href="#geom:section-charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:section-charts">[geom:section-charts]</a>, mapping into it and lying in the simultaneous geometric open of Propositions <a href="#geom:period" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:period">11</a> and <a href="#geom:domination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:domination">15</a>. For any prescribed countable collection of proper closed analytic period loci in the original chart, the following choices are available in this order:* 1. *abelian data $a$ in a residual subset of $A$;* 2. *for each such $a$, an actual section pencil in a residual subset of each prescribed nonempty admissible pencil open meeting $Q$;* 3. *after that pencil is fixed, all but countably many points of its intersection with $Q$ and with a nonempty algebraic good open.* *The selected points avoid the prescribed period loci. The geometric requirements hold on the algebraic good open, and the exceptional points for proper marked rational Hodge-tensor conditions on its whole connected smooth part form a countable set. The pencils in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> have the form $d_t=d_0+td_1$ with $\ell(d_0)=1$ and $\ell(d_1)=0$, and their leading section $d_1$ is smooth and avoids two-torsion. The final countable subset may include any further countable exceptions arising after the pencil is fixed; no uniform exceptional list across pencils is required.
  - Explanation: Abelian data, then the section pencil, then the point on the pencil can be chosen successively so as to avoid prescribed countable analytic period loci.
- **claim-pencil-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-pencil-realization)): Choose the abelian data and the pencil $d_t=d_0+t d_1$ in the generic sets of Lemma <a href="#geom:genericity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:genericity">16</a>. There is a nonempty algebraic open $O\subset\mathbf A^1_t$ and a smooth projective family of K3 surfaces $S^0\to O$ with a flat isometric embedding of rational Hodge variations $$U\ \hookrightarrow\ R^2(S^0/O)_*\mathbb Q.$$ The form on $U$ here is its cup-product form inherited from $Y$. For very general $t$, this summand is the transcendental part of $H^2(S^0_t,\mathbb Q)$. For every $s$ in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of $\mathbf A^1_s$, every smooth projective model of the total family obtained by $t=s+u^2$ is uniruled. The K3 family over $O$ is defined over the original $t$-line; an additional unspecified finite base change is not part of the conclusion.
  - Explanation: A section pencil is realized by a smooth projective K3 family over an open of the original line carrying the rank-eight variation, with uniruled square pullbacks.
- **claim-lattice-enlargement** (construction, [source](#stmt-pencil-lattice)): Let $V$ be an even lattice of signature $(2,14)$ with elementary $2$-primary discriminant group. Suppose a group $G\subset O(V)$ acts trivially on that group. There are an even finite-index overlattice $M$ of $V$, a primitive embedding $M\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}$, and a primitive vector $h\in M^\perp$ with $(h,h)>0$, such that the action of $G$ extends to $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}$ and is the identity on $M^\perp$.
  - Explanation: An even overlattice of small discriminant length embeds primitively in the K3 lattice with a positive class, and the monodromy extends trivially on the complement.
- **claim-pencil-monodromy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pencil-monodromy)): At every finite singular parameter of this general pencil, the monodromy on $V$ is a product of reflections in mutually orthogonal integral $(-2)$-roots in $V$. These roots are perpendicular to the limiting Hodge line. The periods extend to interior periods after killing these finite monodromies. At infinity the monodromy on $V$ is $-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$.
  - Explanation: Finite local monodromies are products of reflections in orthogonal integral roots perpendicular to the limiting Hodge line; at infinity it is minus the identity.
- **claim-period-stack** (construction, [source](#stmt-pencil-periodstack)): Let $\mathscr P_h=[\Gamma_h\backslash\mathscr D_h]$, and let $\mathscr M_h$ denote the moduli stack of primitively polarized ADE K3 surfaces of degree $(h,h)$. There is a morphism $$q:\mathscr P_h\longrightarrow\mathscr M_h$$ whose composition with the period map of quasi-polarized smooth K3 surfaces is their canonical polarized contraction. The pullback of the Hodge line of $\mathscr M_h$ is the tautological period line, including its $\Gamma_h$-linearization.* *For $[\sigma]\in\mathscr D_h$, let $W_\sigma$ be generated by the reflections in the roots $$R_\sigma=\{r\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}:r^2=-2,\ (r,h)=0,\ (r,\sigma)=0\}.$$ The kernel of the homomorphism on stabilizers induced by $q$ is exactly $W_\sigma$.
  - Explanation: The integral period stack maps to the moduli stack of polarized ADE K3 surfaces; the kernel on stabilizers is generated by root reflections.
- **claim-curve-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-pencil-descent)): Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, let $D\subset C$ be finite, and put $C^\circ=C\setminus D$. Suppose the following data are given:* 1. *a local system $\mathcal L$ of lattices isometric to $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}$, with a primitive flat section $h$ of positive square;* 2. *a holomorphic line $\mathcal F\subset\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal O_{C^\circ}$ with $$(\mathcal F,\mathcal F)=0,\qquad (\sigma,\overline\sigma)>0\quad(0\ne\sigma\in\mathcal F),\qquad (\mathcal F,h)=0;$$* 3. *finite local monodromy at every point of $D$, and an interior limit of the period line after a local cover killing that monodromy.* *Then there is a normal projective model $f:\mathcal Z\to C$ whose restriction to $C^\circ$ is a family of polarized ADE K3 surfaces realizing these integral period data modulo contracted roots.* *Suppose, in addition, that the local monodromy at each $c\in D$, viewed on the limiting marked lattice, belongs to the Weyl group generated by $$R_c=\{r\in\mathcal L_c:r^2=-2,\ (r,h)=0,\ (r,\mathcal F_c)=0\}. \tag{\(*\)}\label{pencil:rootcondition}$$ Then $\mathcal Z\to C$ can be chosen to be a flat family of polarized ADE K3 surfaces on all of $C$. Its extended Hodge line descends to a line bundle $\lambda$ on $C$, and $$\omega_{\mathcal Z/C}\simeq f^*\lambda, \qquad K_{\mathcal Z}=f^*(K_C\otimes\lambda). \label{pencil:canonical}$$* *Under hypotheses <span class="upright">(i)</span>–<span class="upright">(iii)</span>, independently of <a href="#pencil:rootcondition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pencil:rootcondition">[pencil:rootcondition]</a>, let $\mathcal U\subset\mathcal L_\mathbb Q$ be a nondegenerate rational sub-local system containing $\mathcal F$, and suppose every flat root which is of type $(1,1)$ on an open set is perpendicular to $\mathcal U$. After shrinking a nonempty algebraic open in $C^\circ$, there is a smooth projective K3 family $\mathcal S^0$ there and an identification of $\mathcal U$ with an isometric Hodge summand of $R^2\mathcal S^0_*\mathbb Q$. Here a flat root means a locally flat section; it need not be a globally invariant vector. No change of the base curve occurs in this conclusion.
  - Explanation: Period data with finite monodromy and interior limits give a normal projective ADE K3 model over the original curve; under the Weyl condition it is flat with a canonical class formula, and a smooth K3 family exists on an open.
- **claim-uniruled-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pencil-uniruledcriterion)): Under the Weyl condition of Lemma <a href="#pencil:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pencil:descent">21</a>, suppose $\deg(K_C\otimes\lambda)<0$. Every smooth projective model of the function field of $\mathcal Z$ is uniruled.
  - Explanation: A negative degree of the twisted canonical class forces non-pseudo-effective canonical class and hence uniruledness of every smooth model.
- **claim-two-substitution-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-twosquares)): Let $O\subset\mathbf A^1_t$ be a nonempty Zariski open set, and let $$f:\mathcal S\longrightarrow O, \qquad g:\mathcal X\longrightarrow O$$ be smooth projective families of connected surfaces, with the fibers of $f$ K3 surfaces. Suppose that the following conditions hold.* 1. *A polarized rational subvariation $\mathscr U\subset R^2f_*\mathbb Q$ of K3 type, with nondegenerate restricted cup product, is given. Its period map is nonconstant, and $U_t=\mathscr U_t$ is simple for all but countably many $t\in O$.* 2. *There are commuting involutions $j,\delta$ of $\mathcal X$ over $O$ such that $$e=\tfrac14(1+j)(1-\delta)$$ annihilates $R^qg_*\mathbb Q$ for $q\ne2$, together with an isomorphism of rational variations of Hodge structure $$\varphi:\mathscr U\xrightarrow{\sim}eR^2g_*\mathbb Q.$$* 3. *For $s$ in a nonempty Zariski open subset of $\mathbf A^1$, the total spaces of the two surface families pulled back by $t=s+u^2$ have smooth projective uniruled threefold models.* *Then there are a nonempty Zariski open set $G\subset O$ and a countable set $E\subset G$ such that, for every $b\in G\setminus E$, a correspondence $$\Theta_b\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S_b\times X_b)_\mathbb Q$$ induces $\varphi_b$ on $U_b$. The open set $G$ and all auxiliary correspondences used before the choice of $b$ can be fixed for the given pencil.
  - Explanation: Two square substitutions yield an algebraic correspondence between a K3 fibre and a surface fibre inducing the given variation isomorphism, for all but countably many parameters.
- **claim-uniruled-h3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-uniruled)): If $Z$ is a smooth projective uniruled threefold over $\mathbb C$, then $H^3(Z)$ is a direct summand of a finite sum of spaces $H^1(C_i)(-1)$, with both maps of the splitting induced by algebraic correspondences. Consequently, every rational Hodge map between the third cohomologies of two such threefolds is algebraic.
  - Explanation: Third cohomology of a uniruled threefold splits algebraically through curve cohomology, so Hodge maps between such third cohomologies are algebraic.
- **claim-affine-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-affine)): Let $B$ be a smooth affine complex curve.* 1. *A bounded complex on $B$ with finite-rank local-system cohomology only in even degrees has a unique splitting as the direct sum of its cohomology sheaves, shifted to their degrees, inducing the identity on each cohomology sheaf.* 2. *If $A$ is a bounded complex with local-system cohomology, then there is a natural exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow H^1(B,\mathcal H^{n-1}A) \longrightarrow \mathbb H^n(B,A) \longrightarrow H^0(B,\mathcal H^nA)\longrightarrow0.$$ For a morphism of such complexes, its action on the left-hand term is determined by its action on $\mathcal H^{n-1}$.
  - Explanation: On an affine curve, complexes with even-degree local-system cohomology split canonically, and a Leray exact sequence has functorial left term.
- **claim-hodge-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-hodgelift)): Let $I$ be a smooth affine curve, let $h_S:E^S\to I$ be a smooth projective K3 family, and let $h_X:E^X\to I$ be a smooth projective surface family with a relative algebraic idempotent $e$ acting on the direct-image complex whose action on $R^q h_{X*}\mathbb Q$ vanishes for $q\ne2$. Suppose that $$a:R^2h_{S*}\mathbb Q\longrightarrow eR^2h_{X*}\mathbb Q$$ is a morphism of rational Hodge variations. Let $Z^S,Z^X$ be smooth projective compactifications which extend the two maps to a smooth projective compactification of $I$ and are unchanged over $I$. Write $\overline I$ for that compactified curve. There is a rational Hodge map $c:H^3(Z^S)\to H^3(Z^X)$ satisfying $$e\,\operatorname{res}_X(cz) =H^1(I,a)\bigl(\operatorname{res}_S z\bigr), \qquad z\in H^3(Z^S),$$ where $H^3(E^S)$ and $eH^3(E^X)$ are identified canonically with $H^1(I,R^2h_{S*}\mathbb Q)$ and $H^1(I,eR^2h_{X*}\mathbb Q)$.
  - Explanation: A morphism of variations over an affine curve lifts to a rational Hodge map between third cohomologies of compactifications.
- **claim-compatible-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-models)): For a smooth projective surface family $\mathcal Y\to O$, its first square pullbacks admit models $Z$ projective over $\mathbf A^1_s$ and a nonempty open set $G\subset O$ such that $Z_G\to G$ is smooth. These models may be chosen with the following additional property. For every $b\in G$ there is a smooth projective fourfold $M_b$, with morphisms to $\mathbf P^1_t$ and $\mathbf P^1_v$, whose inverse images of $V_0\subset\mathbf P^1_v$ and $O\subset\mathbf P^1_t$ are, respectively,* 1. *$Z_G\times_GV_0$, where $V_0\to G$ is $v\mapsto b+v^2$ and $V_0=\{v\in\mathbf A^1:b+v^2\in G\}$;* 2. *$\mathcal Y\times_OQ_b$, where $$\label{comp:conic} Q_b=\{u^2+v^2=(t-b)w^2\}\subset O\times\mathbf P^2.$$* *The two identifications agree on their intersection. In particular, both specified spaces are preserved as actual open subvarieties. Shrinking $G$ before choosing $b$ preserves these conclusions.
  - Explanation: Models of the square pullbacks and fourfolds exist compatibly with the conic chart, with choices fixed before the second substitution.
- **claim-surface-correspondence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-surface)): Fix abelian-cover data and a section pencil for which [geom:structure](#geom:structure), [geom:period](#geom:period), [pencil:realization](#pencil:realization) apply, with nonconstant $U$-period along the pencil and simple $U_t$ outside a countable set. On a nonempty open of that pencil, for all but countably many $b$, there is an algebraic correspondence $$\Theta_b\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S_b^0\times X_b)_\mathbb Q$$ whose action on $U_b$ is the given identification with $H^2(X_b)^{j=+,\,\delta=-}$.
  - Explanation: For the abelian-cover pencil the two-substitution theorem gives an algebraic correspondence realizing the identification on the variation for all but countably many parameters.
- **claim-curve-source** (construction, [source](#stmt-final-curves)): Choose the abelian data and the pencil as in Lemma <a href="#geom:genericity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:genericity">16</a>, and then choose $b$ in the common good open outside the countable exceptional sets of that lemma and Corollary <a href="#comp:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:surface">28</a>. Then there are smooth projective curves $C_1,C_2$ and a rational algebraic correspondence $$Z\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S_b^0\times C_1\times C_2)_{\mathbb Q}$$ whose action restricts to an injection $$f:U_b\longrightarrow H^1(C_1,\mathbb Q)\otimes H^1(C_2,\mathbb Q).$$ Here $U_b=T_{S_b^0}$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Hg}}(U_b)=\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(U_b)$.
  - Explanation: At selected periods the transcendental space of the auxiliary K3 embeds algebraically into the tensor of two curve cohomologies, with full special orthogonal Hodge group.
- **claim-half-spin-spanning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-final-spin)): Let $(U,q)$ be an eight-dimensional polarized rational Hodge structure of K3 type, of signature $(2,6)$, and suppose $\mathop{\mathrm{Hg}}(U)=\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(U,q)$. Let $W_1,W_2$ be polarized effective weight-one Hodge structures and let $W=C^+(U,q)$ have its Kuga–Satake Hodge structure. For every nonzero Hodge map $$f:U\longrightarrow W_1\otimes W_2,$$ put $$\mathcal H=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{HS}}(W_1,W)\otimes_{\mathbb Q} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{HS}}(W_2,W).$$ If $\tau:W\otimes W\to W\otimes W$ is the ordinary tensor flip, the rational linear map $$\Phi:\mathcal H\oplus\mathcal H\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{HS}}(U,W\otimes W),$$ defined by $$\begin{cases} \Phi(a\otimes b,0)=(a\otimes b)\circ f,\\ \Phi(0,a\otimes b)=\tau\circ(a\otimes b)\circ f \end{cases}$$ is surjective.* *If $U\subset H^2(M,\mathbb Q)$ for a smooth projective surface $M$, $W_i=H^1(C_i,\mathbb Q)$, $W=H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$, and $f$ is the restriction of a codimension-two algebraic correspondence from $M$ to $C_1\times C_2$, then every map in the target of $\Phi$ is induced by a cycle in $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(M\times A\times A)_\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: Composing a nonzero Hodge map into the curve tensor with Hodge maps and flips spans all Hodge maps into the tensor square of the full even Clifford module, and these are algebraic when the source is.
- **claim-generic-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-final-generic)): Let $b$ be a construction point of Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a>, and let $S$ be a projective K3 surface equipped with a rational Hodge isometry $r_T:T_S\xrightarrow{\sim}U_b=T_{S_b^0}$ for the cup-product forms. For every full even-Clifford model $A$ of $T_S$, every rational nonisotropic right factor $w$, and every rational polarization $E$, there is a cycle $$\Gamma_S\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A\times A)_\mathbb Q, \qquad (\Gamma_S)_*x=\iota_{w,E}(x)\quad(x\in T_S),$$ where the equality holds in $H^2(A\times A,\mathbb Q)$.
  - Explanation: A rational Hodge isometry to a construction period transfers the curve source, realizing the prescribed tensor by a cycle for any Clifford model, right factor and polarization.
- **claim-buskin-remark** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): Buskin’s theorem does not require integrality or preservation of an ample chamber. In its cyclic reduction, order one means an integral isometry. After a possible sign change and finitely many reflections in algebraic $(-2)$-classes, it maps a Kähler chamber to a Kähler chamber, so strong Torelli realizes it by an isomorphism; its graph and the sign and reflection correspondences are algebraic. In the final rational-reflection chain, transport an initial integral ample class through the partial isometries. On each intermediate period-realized K3 it is a rational $(1,1)$-class of positive square. Clearing denominators gives an integral such class, so the K3 projectivity criterion makes every intermediate surface projective, as required for the cyclic steps.
  - Explanation: The cited isometry-algebraicity theorem needs neither integrality nor a preserved ample chamber, because reflections and sign changes are algebraic and projectivity is preserved along the chain.
- **claim-ambient-tensor** (construction, [source](#stmt-specialization-ambient)): Let $S$ have an ample primitive $P$-polarization and put $U_P=P_{\mathbb Q}^{\perp}\subset H^2(S,\mathbb Q)$. There are a full even-Clifford realization $W_U=C^+(U_P,q)=H^1(A_U,\mathbb Q)$, a rational polarization $E_0$, a nonisotropic rational $w_0\in U_P$, and a cycle $$\Gamma_U\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A_U^2)_{\mathbb Q}$$ whose full degree-two action on every $x\in U_P$ is exactly $\iota_{w_0,E_0}(x)$. This assertion concerns the whole fixed rank-eight space, including its rational $(1,1)$ classes at a Picard jump.
  - Explanation: On the whole fixed rank-eight perpendicular of P, including Picard jumps, one cycle induces exactly one fixed standard tensor.
- **claim-proper-cycle-images** (theorem, [source](#stmt-final-hilbert)): Let $f:\mathcal Z\to B$ be a smooth proper holomorphic family of pure relative dimension $n$ over a connected coordinate ball, with a closed relative embedding $\mathcal Z\subset\mathbf P^N\times B$. Fix an integer $0\leq r\leq n$, a rational local system $\mathcal V$ on $B$, a morphism of local systems $$L:R^{2r}f_*\mathbb Q\longrightarrow\mathcal V,$$ and a flat section $\tau$ of $\mathcal V$. The set of $b\in B$ admitting a rational codimension-$r$ algebraic cycle $Z_b$ such that $$\label{final:cycle-condition} L_b\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{cl}}(Z_b)\bigr)=\tau_b$$ is a countable union of closed analytic subsets of $B$. Each subset in this union is the image of a proper analytic parameter space carrying a fixed rational combination of universal flat subschemes; every point of that space supplies a cycle satisfying <a href="#final:cycle-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="final:cycle-condition">[final:cycle-condition]</a>.* *If the successful set contains a nonempty open subset of $B$, it equals $B$. The same conclusion holds if, for every countable union of proper closed analytic subsets of $B$, there is a successful point outside that union.
  - Explanation: The set of parameters admitting a cycle with a prescribed cohomological action is a countable union of closed analytic images of proper spaces, and equals the base if it contains an open set.
- **claim-rational-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-specialization-rational-density)): Let $(V,q)$ be a rational quadratic space of signature $(2,n)$. The group $O(V)(\mathbb Q)$ is dense in $O(V_{\mathbb R})$, and the rational orbit of every point of its type-IV period domain meets every nonempty open subset of that domain.
  - Explanation: Rational orthogonal transformations are dense, so rational orbits meet every open set of the type-IV period domain.
- **claim-compression-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-compression)): Let $i:W_T\hookrightarrow W_U$ be an inclusion of polarizable rational weight-one Hodge structures, let $p:W_U\to W_T$ be a Hodge retraction, and let $E_U,E_T$ be rational polarizations. Let $H$ be a rational Hodge structure of weight zero. For a Hodge map $j:H\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(W_U)$, write $\iota_{j,E_U}:H(-1)\to W_U\otimes W_U$ for its tensor form. Set $$b=(E_T^\flat)^{-1}i^\vee(-1)E_U^\flat:W_U\longrightarrow W_T.$$ Then $$\label{transfer:compression-identity} (p\otimes b)\iota_{j,E_U}=\iota_{pji,E_T}.$$ If the weight-one structures are abelian $H^1$ spaces, both maps on the tensor slots are induced by rational algebraic correspondences.
  - Explanation: Compressing the ambient tensor by a retraction and a polarization-induced map gives the tensor of the compressed map; the tensor-slot maps on abelian first cohomology are algebraic.
- **claim-ample-tensor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-ample)): Let $S$ be an ample $P$-polarized K3 surface. For every prescribed nonisotropic $w\in T_S$, rational polarization $E$ and transported even-Clifford realization of $C^+(T_S,q)$, its standard tensor $\iota_{w,E}$ has a rational codimension-two algebraic realization.
  - Explanation: On an ample P-polarized K3 surface the standard tensor of the actual transcendental space has a codimension-two realization for all prescribed data.
- **claim-period-isometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-period)): Let $S$ be a projective K3 surface with a rational isometric embedding $\iota:T_S\hookrightarrow V_P$. There is an ample $P$-polarized K3 surface $R$ and a full rational Hodge isometry $$\Phi:H^2(S,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^2(R,\mathbb Q),\qquad \Phi(T_S)=T_R.$$ Both directions of this isometry are induced by algebraic correspondences.
  - Explanation: A surface with a rational isometric embedding into V_P is related to an ample P-polarized surface by a full rational Hodge isometry algebraic in both directions.
- **claim-tensor-algebraic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-tensor)): Under the isometric-embedding hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">2</a>, the exact tensor $\iota_{w,E}$ on $T_S$ is algebraic for every stipulated $w$, $E$, and transported isogenous even-Clifford model.
  - Explanation: Under the embedding hypothesis, the exact tensor is algebraic for every stipulated right factor, polarization and isogenous model.
- **claim-hilbert-moduli** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-moduli)): Let $S$ be a smooth projective complex K3 surface admitting a rational isometric embedding $(T_S,q)\hookrightarrow V_P$. For every $n,r\ge1$, rational HC and rational GHC hold in every degree of $(S^{[n]})^r$, where $S^{[n]}$ is the Hilbert scheme of length-$n$ subschemes of $S$.* *More generally, let $\alpha\in\operatorname{Br}(S)$ and let $M$ be either a smooth projective moduli space of Gieseker-stable $\alpha$-twisted sheaves on $S$, or a smooth projective moduli space of $\sigma$-stable objects in $D^b(S,\alpha)$ for a generic Bridgeland stability condition $\sigma$. Then rational HC and rational GHC hold in every degree of $M^r$ for every $r\ge1$. If $M$ is hyperkähler, the same conclusions hold for every smooth projective hyperkähler variety birational to $M$ and all its self-powers.
  - Explanation: HC and GHC pass to self-powers of Hilbert schemes of points and to the specified moduli spaces and birational hyperkähler varieties through motivic decompositions.

```json
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ satisfy the exact even-Clifford algebraicity hypothesis of Proposition <a href=\"#ext:conditional\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:conditional\">4</a>, and put $t=\\dim_\\mathbb QT$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ghc-lem-orthogonal-carriers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V=T(1)$ for a projective K3 surface with $\\operatorname{Hg}(V)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(V,q)$ and $t=\\dim V\\ge3$. Let $H$ be a nontrivial rational simple representation occurring in a direct sum of tensor powers of $V$, endowed with its induced weight-zero Hodge structure. If its Hodge level is at most $2b$, there are integers $c,s\\ge1$ and $k_1,\\ldots,k_c\\ge1$ such that $$c\\le b,\\qquad s=\\sum_i k_i,\\qquad k_i\\le t,\n \\qquad \\bigotimes_{i=1}^c\\bigwedge^{k_i}V\\longrightarrow H$$ is a surjective rational Hodge map.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ghc-lem-orthogonal-carriers",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V=T(1)$ for a projective K3 surface with $\\operatorname{Hg}(V)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(V,q)$ and $t=\\dim V\\ge3$.           "source_id": "PM-032.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-structure",
        "exact_quote": "There is a smooth double cover $Y\\to K=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Km}}(B')$, with $K$ the resolved Kummer surface, satisfying the following assertions.*\n\n1.  *The anti-invariant lattice $$V=H^2(Y,\\mathbb Z)^{\\delta=-1}$$ (with torsion removed) is even, has signature $(2,14)$, and has $2$-elementary discriminant group. For fixed $(B,M,\\chi)$, monodromy acts trivially on this discriminant group.*\n\n2.  *There are eight mutually orthogonal algebraic anti-invariant classes $a_1,\\ldots,a_8$, each of square $-4$. Their perpendicular $$U=\\langle a_1,\\ldots,a_8\\rangle^\\perp\\subset V_\\mathbb Q$$ is a Hodge structure of signature $(2,6)$ and, with the intersection form inherited from $Y$, is rationally isometric to $$V_P=(\\mathbb U\\oplus\\mathbb U(2)\\oplus D_4(-1))_\\mathbb Q.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Resolved pullback gives a flat Hodge isomorphism $\\beta:U\\xrightarrow{\\sim}U_X$ with $$(\\beta x,\\beta y)_X=2(x,y)_Y.$$ The algebraic projector $$\\label{geom:projector}\n     e=\\frac14(\\Delta_X+\\Gamma_j-\\Gamma_\\delta-\\Gamma_{j\\delta})\n           \\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{CH}}^2(X\\times X)_\\mathbb Q$$ has image $U_X$ in degree two and is zero in every other degree.*\n\n*The constructions are compatible with variation on the indicated smooth parameter opens.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-structure",
          "exact_quote": "There is a smooth double cover $Y\\to K=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Km}}(B')$, with $K$ the resolved Kummer surface, satisfying the following assertions.*\n\n1.  *The anti-invariant lattice $$V=H^2(Y,\\mathbb Z)^{\\delta=-1}$$ (with torsion removed) is even, has signature $(2,14)$, and has $2$-elementary discriminant group. For fixed $(B,M,\\chi)$, monodromy acts trivially on this discriminant group.*\n\n2.  *There are eight mutually orthogonal algebraic anti-invariant classes $a_1,\\ldots,a_8$, each of square $-4$. Their perpendicular $$U=\\langle a_1,\\ldots,a_8\\rangle^\\perp\\subset V_\\mathbb Q$$ is a Hodge structure of signature $(2,6)$ and, with the intersection form inherited from $Y$, is rationally isometric to $$V_P=(\\mathbb U\\oplus\\mathbb U(2)\\oplus D_4(-1))_\\mathbb Q.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Resolved pullback gives a flat Hodge isomorphism $\\beta:U\\xrightarrow{\\sim}U_X$ with $$(\\beta x,\\beta y)_X=2(x,y)_Y.$$ The algebraic projector $$\\label{geom:projector}\n     e=\\frac14(\\Delta_X+\\Gamma_j-\\Gamma_\\delta-\\Gamma_{j\\delta})\n           \\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{CH}}^2(X\\times X)_\\mathbb Q$$ has image $U_X$ in degree two and is zero in every other degree.*\n\n*The constructions are compatible with variation on the indicated smooth parameter opens.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There is a smooth double cover $Y\\to K=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Km}}(B')$, with $K$ the resolved Kummer surface, satisfying the following assertions.*\n\n1.  *The anti-invariant lattice $$V=H^2(Y,\\mathbb Z)^{\\delta=-1}$$ (with torsion removed) is even, has signature $(2,14)$, and has $2$-elementary discriminant group.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-twisted-hodge",
        "exact_quote": "Put $E=T_B(-\\log D)$. There is a flat complex identification of $U_X$ with a character eigenspace having Hodge components $$\\label{geom:hodge-eigensheaves}\n U_X^{p,q}\\simeq\n H^q\\bigl(B,\\Omega_B^p(\\log D)\\otimes M^{-1}\\chi\\bigr),\n \\qquad p+q=2.$$ In particular, $U_X^{2,0}=H^0(B,M\\chi)$ is one-dimensional. Fix a nonzero translation-invariant volume form $\\Omega$ on $B$, and let $\\theta\\in H^0(B,M\\chi)$ represent this line. Under <a href=\"#geom:hodge-eigensheaves\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:hodge-eigensheaves\">[geom:hodge-eigensheaves]</a>, the period differential is the map on $H^1$ induced by $$\\label{geom:derivative}\n E\\xrightarrow{\\ \\theta\\ }E\\otimes M\\chi\n       \\simeq\\Omega_B^1(\\log D)\\otimes M^{-1}\\chi.$$ The Kodaira–Spencer map identifies the tangent space of the full six-dimensional family $(B,M,\\chi,[d])$ with $H^1(B,E)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-geom-gauss-generic",
        "exact_quote": "For $(B,D)$ in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of the parameter space, $D$ has no nontrivial translation stabilizer. Its quotient $C=D/\\{\\pm1\\}$ is a smooth genus-three curve, and the connected étale cover $D\\to C$ is defined by a nontrivial line bundle $\\epsilon\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[2]$. The Gauss map is the composite $$D\\longrightarrow C\\xrightarrow{|K_C\\otimes\\epsilon|}\\mathbf P^1,$$ of degrees two and four. No fiber of the degree-four map has all its multiplicities even. The good open remains nonempty on every connected component obtained by adjoining the finite theta, torsion, and level choices used to define $X$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-halves",
        "exact_quote": "There is an algebraic map $h:\\mathcal S\\to B$ such that $$\\label{geom:coherent}\n 2h(s)=\\sum_{p\\in s}p,\\qquad h(s[p])=h(s)-p.$$ The selections split globally into two degree-eight covers $\\mathcal S_+\\sqcup\\mathcal S_-$ according to the parity of the number of sign changes. Their smooth projective completions $C_\\pm$, which may be disconnected, carry extensions $a_\\pm:C_\\pm\\to B$ of $h$. Wherever its differential is nonzero, either map is tangent to the direction recorded by its parameter in $\\mathbf P^1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-geom-relations",
        "exact_quote": "On a regular-value disc, no signed sum of a nonempty subset of the four local branches $x_i$ is constant.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-relations",
          "exact_quote": "On a regular-value disc, no signed sum of a nonempty subset of the four local branches $x_i$ is constant. Every connected component of $C_+$ and $C_-$ maps nonconstantly to $B$. No image component from $C_+$ is an opposite translate of an image component from $C_-$; that is, their image curves cannot satisfy $Z_+=b-Z_-$ for any $b\\in B$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-domination",
        "exact_quote": "For every parameter in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of the parameter space, there exist smooth projective connected curves $C_1,C_2$ and a dominant generically finite morphism $$g:C_1\\times C_2\\longrightarrow X.$$ Whenever additionally $U_X$ has no nonzero rational $(1,1)$-class, an algebraic correspondence induces an injection $$U_X\\hookrightarrow H^1(C_1,\\mathbb Q)\\otimes H^1(C_2,\\mathbb Q).$$ In particular, this injection exists at a very general parameter.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-032.2"
        }

      "explanation": "The degree-eight addition map lifts to a dominant generically finite map from a product of curves onto the double cover, giving an algebraic injection into the tensor of curve cohomologies.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-genericity",
        "exact_quote": "Every nonempty regular period chart admits a smaller normalized product chart $A\\times Q$ of abelian data and affine section coordinates as in Section <a href=\"#geom:section-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:section-charts\">[geom:section-charts]</a>, mapping into it and lying in the simultaneous geometric open of Propositions <a href=\"#geom:period\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:period\">11</a> and <a href=\"#geom:domination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:domination\">15</a>. For any prescribed countable collection of proper closed analytic period loci in the original chart, the following choices are available in this order:*\n\n1.  *abelian data $a$ in a residual subset of $A$;*\n\n2.  *for each such $a$, an actual section pencil in a residual subset of each prescribed nonempty admissible pencil open meeting $Q$;*\n\n3.  *after that pencil is fixed, all but countably many points of its intersection with $Q$ and with a nonempty algebraic good open.*\n\n*The selected points avoid the prescribed period loci. The geometric requirements hold on the algebraic good open, and the exceptional points for proper marked rational Hodge-tensor conditions on its whole connected smooth part form a countable set. The pencils in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> have the form $d_t=d_0+td_1$ with $\\ell(d_0)=1$ and $\\ell(d_1)=0$, and their leading section $d_1$ is smooth and avoids two-torsion. The final countable subset may include any further countable exceptions arising after the pencil is fixed; no uniform exceptional list across pencils is required.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-pencil-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the abelian data and the pencil $d_t=d_0+t d_1$ in the generic sets of Lemma <a href=\"#geom:genericity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:genericity\">16</a>. There is a nonempty algebraic open $O\\subset\\mathbf A^1_t$ and a smooth projective family of K3 surfaces $S^0\\to O$ with a flat isometric embedding of rational Hodge variations $$U\\ \\hookrightarrow\\ R^2(S^0/O)_*\\mathbb Q.$$ The form on $U$ here is its cup-product form inherited from $Y$. For very general $t$, this summand is the transcendental part of $H^2(S^0_t,\\mathbb Q)$. For every $s$ in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of $\\mathbf A^1_s$, every smooth projective model of the total family obtained by $t=s+u^2$ is uniruled. The K3 family over $O$ is defined over the original $t$-line; an additional unspecified finite base change is not part of the conclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-pencil-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be an even lattice of signature $(2,14)$ with elementary $2$-primary discriminant group. Suppose a group $G\\subset O(V)$ acts trivially on that group.         "exact_quote": "At every finite singular parameter of this general pencil, the monodromy on $V$ is a product of reflections in mutually orthogonal integral $(-2)$-roots in $V$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr P_h=[\\Gamma_h\\backslash\\mathscr D_h]$, and let $\\mathscr M_h$ denote the moduli stack of primitively polarized ADE K3 surfaces of degree $(h,h)$. There is a morphism $$q:\\mathscr P_h\\longrightarrow\\mathscr M_h$$ whose composition with the period map of quasi-polarized smooth K3 surfaces is their canonical polarized contraction. The pullback of the Hodge line of $\\mathscr M_h$ is the tautological period line, including its $\\Gamma_h$-linearization.*\n\n*For $[\\sigma]\\in\\mathscr D_h$, let $W_\\sigma$ be generated by the reflections in the roots $$R_\\sigma=\\{r\\in\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{K3}}:r^2=-2,\\ (r,h)=0,\\ (r,\\sigma)=0\\}.$$ The kernel of the homomorphism on stabilizers induced by $q$ is exactly $W_\\sigma$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pencil-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, let $D\\subset C$ be finite, and put $C^\\circ=C\\setminus D$. Suppose the following data are given:*\n\n1.  *a local system $\\mathcal L$ of lattices isometric to $\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{K3}}$, with a primitive flat section $h$ of positive square;*\n\n2.  *a holomorphic line $\\mathcal F\\subset\\mathcal L\\otimes\\mathcal O_{C^\\circ}$ with $$(\\mathcal F,\\mathcal F)=0,\\qquad\n     (\\sigma,\\overline\\sigma)>0\\quad(0\\ne\\sigma\\in\\mathcal F),\\qquad\n     (\\mathcal F,h)=0;$$*\n\n3.  *finite local monodromy at every point of $D$, and an interior limit of the period line after a local cover killing that monodromy.*\n\n*Then there is a normal projective model $f:\\mathcal Z\\to C$ whose restriction to $C^\\circ$ is a family of polarized ADE K3 surfaces realizing these integral period data modulo contracted roots.*\n\n\n\n*Suppose, in addition, that the local monodromy at each $c\\in D$, viewed on the limiting marked lattice, belongs to the Weyl group generated by $$R_c=\\{r\\in\\mathcal L_c:r^2=-2,\\ (r,h)=0,\\ (r,\\mathcal F_c)=0\\}.\n \\tag{\\(*\\)}\\label{pencil:rootcondition}$$ Then $\\mathcal Z\\to C$ can be chosen to be a flat family of polarized ADE K3 surfaces on all of $C$. Its extended Hodge line descends to a line bundle $\\lambda$ on $C$, and $$\\omega_{\\mathcal Z/C}\\simeq f^*\\lambda,\n \\qquad K_{\\mathcal Z}=f^*(K_C\\otimes\\lambda).\n \\label{pencil:canonical}$$*\n\n*Under hypotheses <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>–<span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span>, independently of <a href=\"#pencil:rootcondition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pencil:rootcondition\">[pencil:rootcondition]</a>, let $\\mathcal U\\subset\\mathcal L_\\mathbb Q$ be a nondegenerate rational sub-local system containing $\\mathcal F$, and suppose every flat root which is of type $(1,1)$ on an open set is perpendicular to $\\mathcal U$. After shrinking a nonempty algebraic open in $C^\\circ$, there is a smooth projective K3 family $\\mathcal S^0$ there and an identification of $\\mathcal U$ with an isometric Hodge summand of $R^2\\mathcal S^0_*\\mathbb Q$. Here a flat root means a locally flat section; it need not be a globally invariant vector. No change of the base curve occurs in this conclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pencil-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, let $D\\subset C$ be finite, and put $C^\\circ=C\\setminus D$. Its extended Hodge line descends to a line bundle $\\lambda$ on $C$, and $$\\omega_{\\mathcal Z/C}\\simeq f^*\\lambda,\n \\qquad K_{\\mathcal Z}=f^*(K_C\\otimes\\lambda).\n \\label{pencil:canonical}$$*\n\n*Under hypotheses <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>–<span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span>, independently of <a href=\"#pencil:rootcondition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pencil:rootcondition\">[pencil:rootcondition]</a>, let $\\mathcal U\\subset\\mathcal L_\\mathbb Q$ be a nondegenerate rational sub-local system containing $\\mathcal F$, and suppose every flat root which is of type $(1,1)$ on an open set is perpendicular to $\\mathcal U$.       ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, let $D\\subset C$ be finite, and put $C^\\circ=C\\setminus D$. Suppose the following data are given:*\n\n1.  *a local system $\\mathcal L$ of lattices isometric to $\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{K3}}$, with a primitive flat section $h$ of positive square;*\n\n2.  *a holomorphic line $\\mathcal F\\subset\\mathcal L\\otimes\\mathcal O_{C^\\circ}$ with $$(\\mathcal F,\\mathcal F)=0,\\qquad\n     (\\sigma,\\overline\\sigma)>0\\quad(0\\ne\\sigma\\in\\mathcal F),\\qquad\n     (\\mathcal F,h)=0;$$*\n\n3.  *finite local monodromy at every point of $D$, and an interior limit of the period line after a local cover killing that monodromy.*\n\n*Then there is a normal projective model $f:\\mathcal Z\\to C$ whose restriction to $C^\\circ$ is a family of polarized ADE K3 surfaces realizing these integral period data modulo contracted roots.*\n\n\n\n*Suppose, in addition, that the local monodromy at each $c\\in D$, viewed on the limiting marked lattice, belongs to the Weyl group generated by $$R_c=\\{r\\in\\mathcal L_c:r^2=-2,\\ (r,h)=0,\\ (r,\\mathcal F_c)=0\\}.\n \\tag{\\(*\\)}\\label{pencil:rootcondition}$$ Then $\\mathcal Z\\to C$ can be chosen to be a flat family of polarized ADE K3 surfaces on all of $C$. Its extended Hodge line descends to a line bundle $\\lambda$ on $C$, and $$\\omega_{\\mathcal Z/C}\\simeq f^*\\lambda,\n \\qquad K_{\\mathcal Z}=f^*(K_C\\otimes\\lambda).\n \\label{pencil:canonical}$$*\n\n*Under hypotheses <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>–<span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span>, independently of <a href=\"#pencil:rootcondition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pencil:rootcondition\">[pencil:rootcondition]</a>, let $\\mathcal U\\subset\\mathcal L_\\mathbb Q$ be a nondegenerate rational sub-local system containing $\\mathcal F$, and suppose every flat root which is of type $(1,1)$ on an open set is perpendicular to $\\mathcal U$. After shrinking a nonempty algebraic open in $C^\\circ$, there is a smooth projective K3 family $\\mathcal S^0$ there and an identification of $\\mathcal U$ with an isometric Hodge summand of $R^2\\mathcal S^0_*\\mathbb Q$. Here a flat root means a locally flat section; it need not be a globally invariant vector. No change of the base curve occurs in this conclusion.",
      "explanation": "Period data with finite monodromy and interior limits give a normal projective ADE K3 model over the original curve; under the Weyl condition it is flat with a canonical class formula, and a smooth K3 family exists on an open.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pencil-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, let $D\\subset C$ be finite, and put $C^\\circ=C\\setminus D$.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-twosquares",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O\\subset\\mathbf A^1_t$ be a nonempty Zariski open set, and let $$f:\\mathcal S\\longrightarrow O,\n \\qquad g:\\mathcal X\\longrightarrow O$$ be smooth projective families of connected surfaces, with the fibers of $f$ K3 surfaces. Suppose that the following conditions hold.*\n\n1.  *A polarized rational subvariation $\\mathscr U\\subset R^2f_*\\mathbb Q$ of K3 type, with nondegenerate restricted cup product, is given. Its period map is nonconstant, and $U_t=\\mathscr U_t$ is simple for all but countably many $t\\in O$.*\n\n2.  *There are commuting involutions $j,\\delta$ of $\\mathcal X$ over $O$ such that $$e=\\tfrac14(1+j)(1-\\delta)$$ annihilates $R^qg_*\\mathbb Q$ for $q\\ne2$, together with an isomorphism of rational variations of Hodge structure $$\\varphi:\\mathscr U\\xrightarrow{\\sim}eR^2g_*\\mathbb Q.$$*\n\n3.  *For $s$ in a nonempty Zariski open subset of $\\mathbf A^1$, the total spaces of the two surface families pulled back by $t=s+u^2$ have smooth projective uniruled threefold models.*\n\n*Then there are a nonempty Zariski open set $G\\subset O$ and a countable set $E\\subset G$ such that, for every $b\\in G\\setminus E$, a correspondence $$\\Theta_b\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{CH}}^2(S_b\\times X_b)_\\mathbb Q$$ induces $\\varphi_b$ on $U_b$. The open set $G$ and all auxiliary correspondences used before the choice of $b$ can be fixed for the given pencil.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-affine",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a smooth affine complex curve.*\n\n1.  *A bounded complex on $B$ with finite-rank local-system cohomology only in even degrees has a unique splitting as the direct sum of its cohomology sheaves, shifted to their degrees, inducing the identity on each cohomology sheaf.*\n\n2.  *If $A$ is a bounded complex with local-system cohomology, then there is a natural exact sequence $$0\\longrightarrow H^1(B,\\mathcal H^{n-1}A)\n     \\longrightarrow \\mathbb H^n(B,A)\n     \\longrightarrow H^0(B,\\mathcal H^nA)\\longrightarrow0.$$ For a morphism of such complexes, its action on the left-hand term is determined by its action on $\\mathcal H^{n-1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-032.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $B$ be a smooth affine complex curve.*\n\n1.  *A bounded complex on $B$ with finite-rank local-system cohomology only in even degrees has a unique splitting as the direct sum of its cohomology sheaves, shifted to their degrees, inducing the identity on each cohomology sheaf.*\n\n2.  *If $A$ is a bounded complex with local-system cohomology, then there is a natural exact sequence $$0\\longrightarrow H^1(B,\\mathcal H^{n-1}A)\n     \\longrightarrow \\mathbb H^n(B,A)\n     \\longrightarrow H^0(B,\\mathcal H^nA)\\longrightarrow0.$$ For a morphism of such complexes, its action on the left-hand term is determined by its action on $\\mathcal H^{n-1}$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-hodgelift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be a smooth affine curve, let $h_S:E^S\\to I$ be a smooth projective K3 family, and let $h_X:E^X\\to I$ be a smooth projective surface family with a relative algebraic idempotent $e$ acting on the direct-image complex whose action on $R^q h_{X*}\\mathbb Q$ vanishes for $q\\ne2$. Suppose that $$a:R^2h_{S*}\\mathbb Q\\longrightarrow eR^2h_{X*}\\mathbb Q$$ is a morphism of rational Hodge variations. Let $Z^S,Z^X$ be smooth projective compactifications which extend the two maps to a smooth projective compactification of $I$ and are unchanged over $I$. Write $\\overline I$ for that compactified curve. There is a rational Hodge map $c:H^3(Z^S)\\to H^3(Z^X)$ satisfying $$e\\,\\operatorname{res}_X(cz)\n   =H^1(I,a)\\bigl(\\operatorname{res}_S z\\bigr),\n \\qquad z\\in H^3(Z^S),$$ where $H^3(E^S)$ and $eH^3(E^X)$ are identified canonically with $H^1(I,R^2h_{S*}\\mathbb Q)$ and $H^1(I,eR^2h_{X*}\\mathbb Q)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be a smooth affine curve, let $h_S:E^S\\to I$ be a smooth projective K3 family, and let $h_X:E^X\\to I$ be a smooth projective surface family with a relative algebraic idempotent $e$ acting on the direct-image complex whose action on $R^q h_{X*}\\mathbb Q$ vanishes for $q\\ne2$. Suppose that $$a:R^2h_{S*}\\mathbb Q\\longrightarrow eR^2h_{X*}\\mathbb Q$$ is a morphism of rational Hodge variations. Let $Z^S,Z^X$ be smooth projective compactifications which extend the two maps to a smooth projective compactification of $I$ and are unchanged over $I$. Write $\\overline I$ for that compactified curve. There is a rational Hodge map $c:H^3(Z^S)\\to H^3(Z^X)$ satisfying $$e\\,\\operatorname{res}_X(cz)\n   =H^1(I,a)\\bigl(\\operatorname{res}_S z\\bigr),\n \\qquad z\\in H^3(Z^S),$$ where $H^3(E^S)$ and $eH^3(E^X)$ are identified canonically with $H^1(I,R^2h_{S*}\\mathbb Q)$ and $H^1(I,eR^2h_{X*}\\mathbb Q)$.",

        "exact_quote": "Choose the abelian data and the pencil as in Lemma <a href=\"#geom:genericity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:genericity\">16</a>, and then choose $b$ in the common good open outside the countable exceptional sets of that lemma and Corollary <a href=\"#comp:surface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:surface\">28</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-final-spin",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(U,q)$ be an eight-dimensional polarized rational Hodge structure of K3 type, of signature $(2,6)$, and suppose $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hg}}(U)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(U,q)$. Let $W_1,W_2$ be polarized effective weight-one Hodge structures and let $W=C^+(U,q)$ have its Kuga–Satake Hodge structure. For every nonzero Hodge map $$f:U\\longrightarrow W_1\\otimes W_2,$$ put $$\\mathcal H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{HS}}(W_1,W)\\otimes_{\\mathbb Q}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{HS}}(W_2,W).$$ If $\\tau:W\\otimes W\\to W\\otimes W$ is the ordinary tensor flip, the rational linear map $$\\Phi:\\mathcal H\\oplus\\mathcal H\\longrightarrow\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{HS}}(U,W\\otimes W),$$ defined by $$\\begin{cases}\n \\Phi(a\\otimes b,0)=(a\\otimes b)\\circ f,\\\\\n \\Phi(0,a\\otimes b)=\\tau\\circ(a\\otimes b)\\circ f\n \\end{cases}$$ is surjective.*\n\n*If $U\\subset H^2(M,\\mathbb Q)$ for a smooth projective surface $M$, $W_i=H^1(C_i,\\mathbb Q)$, $W=H^1(A,\\mathbb Q)$, and $f$ is the restriction of a codimension-two algebraic correspondence from $M$ to $C_1\\times C_2$, then every map in the target of $\\Phi$ is induced by a cycle in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{CH}}^2(M\\times A\\times A)_\\mathbb Q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-final-generic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b$ be a construction point of Lemma <a href=\"#final:curves\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"final:curves\">29</a>, and let $S$ be a projective K3 surface equipped with a rational Hodge isometry $r_T:T_S\\xrightarrow{\\sim}U_b=T_{S_b^0}$ for the cup-product forms.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
        "exact_quote": "Buskin’s theorem does not require integrality or preservation of an ample chamber. In its cyclic reduction, order one means an integral isometry. After a possible sign change and finitely many reflections in algebraic $(-2)$-classes, it maps a Kähler chamber to a Kähler chamber, so strong Torelli realizes it by an isomorphism; its graph and the sign and reflection correspondences are algebraic. In the final rational-reflection chain, transport an initial integral ample class through the partial isometries. On each intermediate period-realized K3 it is a rational $(1,1)$-class of positive square. Clearing denominators gives an integral such class, so the K3 projectivity criterion makes every intermediate surface projective, as required for the cyclic steps.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
          "exact_quote": "Buskin’s theorem does not require integrality or preservation of an ample chamber. In its cyclic reduction, order one means an integral isometry. On each intermediate period-realized K3 it is a rational $(1,1)$-class of positive square.           "source_id": "PM-032.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-final-hilbert",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\mathcal Z\\to B$ be a smooth proper holomorphic family of pure relative dimension $n$ over a connected coordinate ball, with a closed relative embedding $\\mathcal Z\\subset\\mathbf P^N\\times B$. Fix an integer $0\\leq r\\leq n$, a rational local system $\\mathcal V$ on $B$, a morphism of local systems $$L:R^{2r}f_*\\mathbb Q\\longrightarrow\\mathcal V,$$ and a flat section $\\tau$ of $\\mathcal V$. The set of $b\\in B$ admitting a rational codimension-$r$ algebraic cycle $Z_b$ such that $$\\label{final:cycle-condition}\n L_b\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{cl}}(Z_b)\\bigr)=\\tau_b$$ is a countable union of closed analytic subsets of $B$. Each subset in this union is the image of a proper analytic parameter space carrying a fixed rational combination of universal flat subschemes; every point of that space supplies a cycle satisfying <a href=\"#final:cycle-condition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"final:cycle-condition\">[final:cycle-condition]</a>.*\n\n*If the successful set contains a nonempty open subset of $B$, it equals $B$. The same conclusion holds if, for every countable union of proper closed analytic subsets of $B$, there is a successful point outside that union.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },

The set of $b\\in B$ admitting a rational codimension-$r$ algebraic cycle $Z_b$ such that $$\\label{final:cycle-condition}\n L_b\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{cl}}(Z_b)\\bigr)=\\tau_b$$ is a countable union of closed analytic subsets of $B$. Each subset in this union is the image of a proper analytic parameter space carrying a fixed rational combination of universal flat subschemes; every point of that space supplies a cycle satisfying <a href=\"#final:cycle-condition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"final:cycle-condition\">[final:cycle-condition]</a>.*\n\n*If the successful set contains a nonempty open subset of $B$, it equals $B$.         "exact_quote": "Let $(V,q)$ be a rational quadratic space of signature $(2,n)$. The group $O(V)(\\mathbb Q)$ is dense in $O(V_{\\mathbb R})$, and the rational orbit of every point of its type-IV period domain meets every nonempty open subset of that domain.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-transfer-compression",
        "exact_quote": "Let $i:W_T\\hookrightarrow W_U$ be an inclusion of polarizable rational weight-one Hodge structures, let $p:W_U\\to W_T$ be a Hodge retraction, and let $E_U,E_T$ be rational polarizations. Let $H$ be a rational Hodge structure of weight zero. For a Hodge map $j:H\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(W_U)$, write $\\iota_{j,E_U}:H(-1)\\to W_U\\otimes W_U$ for its tensor form. Set $$b=(E_T^\\flat)^{-1}i^\\vee(-1)E_U^\\flat:W_U\\longrightarrow W_T.$$ Then $$\\label{transfer:compression-identity}\n (p\\otimes b)\\iota_{j,E_U}=\\iota_{pji,E_T}.$$ If the weight-one structures are abelian $H^1$ spaces, both maps on the tensor slots are induced by rational algebraic correspondences.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-transfer-compression",
          "exact_quote": "Let $i:W_T\\hookrightarrow W_U$ be an inclusion of polarizable rational weight-one Hodge structures, let $p:W_U\\to W_T$ be a Hodge retraction, and let $E_U,E_T$ be rational polarizations. Let $H$ be a rational Hodge structure of weight zero. For a Hodge map $j:H\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(W_U)$, write $\\iota_{j,E_U}:H(-1)\\to W_U\\otimes W_U$ for its tensor form. Set $$b=(E_T^\\flat)^{-1}i^\\vee(-1)E_U^\\flat:W_U\\longrightarrow W_T.$$ Then $$\\label{transfer:compression-identity}\n (p\\otimes b)\\iota_{j,E_U}=\\iota_{pji,E_T}.$$ If the weight-one structures are abelian $H^1$ spaces, both maps on the tensor slots are induced by rational algebraic correspondences.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.2"
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **ample P-polarization** ([source](#pm-h-4)): An *ample $P$-polarization* on $S$ means a primitive embedding $P\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(S)$ whose image contains an ample class.
- **standard even-Clifford Kuga–Satake structure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its full even algebra $W=C^+(T,q)$ carries the standard left Kuga–Satake Hodge structure.
- **selection** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A *selection* means an unordered subset containing exactly one point of each opposite pair.
- **residual set** ([source](#pm-h-23)): We call a set residual if its complement is a countable union of closed nowhere-dense subsets.
- **polarized ADE K3 surface** ([source](#pm-h-27)): A *polarized ADE K3 surface* is a normal surface with rational double points whose minimal resolution is a K3 surface, together with a primitive ample polarization.
- **Hodge level** ([source](#ghc:subsec:k3-ghc)): The Hodge level of a nonzero pure Hodge structure is $\max\{\|a-b\|:H^{a,b}\ne0\}$.
- **Hodge coniveau** ([source](#ghc:subsec:exterior-coniveau)): Recall that a sub-Hodge structure has Hodge coniveau at least $p$ when all its types $(a,b)$ satisfy $a,b\ge p$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-lattice** [claim](#stmt-main-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-lattice): Discriminant-form gluing then Witt extension -> Primitive embedding of P with complement isometric to V_P
- **r-exterior-returns** [claim](#stmt-ext-conditional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-conditional): Alternate copies of the Clifford map, contract by polarization, invert by Cayley–Hamilton polynomial -> Algebraic injections and left inverses for exterior powers
- **r-cycle-support** [claim](#stmt-ghc-lem-cycle-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ghc-lem-cycle-support): Dimension count of the cycle support -> Image of a correspondence lies in coniveau p
- **r-exterior-coniveau** [claim](#stmt-ghc-prop-exterior-coniveau) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ghc-prop-exterior-coniveau): Apply support lemma to the return correspondence -> Exterior powers lie in coniveau k-1
- **r-hodge-ordinary** [claim](#stmt-ext-criterion) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Invariant theory of the Hodge group with algebraic projectors and Künneth assembly -> Ordinary HC on all self-powers
- **r-orthogonal-carriers** [claim](#stmt-ghc-lem-orthogonal-carriers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ghc-lem-orthogonal-carriers): Highest-weight columns and rational descent of intertwiners -> Surjection from exterior carriers onto bounded-level representations
- **r-k3-carriers** [claim](#stmt-ghc-lem-k3-carriers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ghc-lem-k3-carriers): Galois transport of widths for real and CM cases -> Carrier bound for all endomorphism fields
- **r-ghc-assembly** [claim](#stmt-ext-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ghc-lem-k3-carriers-2): Compose returns with an HC-realized quotient map and bound the support dimension -> GHC on all self-powers
- **r-double-cover** [claim](#stmt-geom-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-structure): Local fibre-sign analysis, lattice gluing and local-global classification of quadratic forms -> Eigenspace isometric to V_P with algebraic projector
- **r-twisted-hodge** [claim](#stmt-geom-twisted-hodge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-twisted-hodge): Character eigensheaves of the double cover and logarithmic normal sequence -> Period differential and Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism
- **r-period-rank** [claim](#stmt-geom-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-period): Tangency matrix on the theta curve and an invertibility test -> Full-rank period map and full Hodge group at very general points
- **r-gauss-generic** [claim](#stmt-geom-gauss-generic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-gauss-generic): Dominance of the parameter map to a moduli space of curves with two-torsion and a closed all-even condition -> Genus-three quotient and twisted canonical pencil
- **r-coherent-halves** [claim](#stmt-geom-halves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-halves): Trivial-monodromy torsor of coherent assignments -> Parity covers and half-sum maps
- **r-relations** [claim](#stmt-geom-relations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-relations): Monodromy and isogeny arguments excluding signed relations -> Nonconstant, non-opposite image curves
- **r-domination** [claim](#stmt-geom-domination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-domination): Degree count, even pullback divisor, lift through the étale double cover of the product -> Dominant map from a curve product and algebraic injection into the tensor of curve cohomologies
- **r-genericity** [claim](#stmt-geom-genericity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-genericity): Baire category over product charts -> Ordered choice of data, pencil and point
- **r-pencil-lattice** [claim](#stmt-pencil-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-lattice): Adjoin isotropic discriminant vectors, then primitive-embedding criterion -> Overlattice in the K3 lattice with extended monodromy
- **r-pencil-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-pencil-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-monodromy): Local Picard–Lefschetz analysis at nodes and fixed points, simultaneous resolution -> Monodromy as a product of orthogonal root reflections
- **r-period-stack** [claim](#stmt-pencil-periodstack) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-periodstack): Contraction morphism and Torelli on root mirrors -> Map from the period stack to ADE moduli with Weyl kernel
- **r-descent** [claim](#stmt-pencil-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-descent): Finite Galois cover, GAGA algebraization and invariant descent -> Normal projective family over the original curve and canonical class formula
- **r-uniruled-criterion** [claim](#stmt-pencil-uniruledcriterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-uniruledcriterion): Intersection with a nef class and non-pseudo-effectivity criterion -> Uniruledness of every smooth model
- **r-pencil-realization** [claim](#stmt-pencil-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-uniruledcriterion-2): Quadratic substitution kills the sign at infinity; Hodge line of degree one -> K3 family carrying the variation, with uniruled pullbacks
- **r-uniruled-h3** [claim](#stmt-comp-uniruled) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-uniruled): Dominant map from a surface times a line, blowup formulas, divisor splittings -> Algebraic third-cohomology Hodge maps
- **r-hodge-lift** [claim](#stmt-comp-hodgelift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-hodgelift): Global invariant cycle theorem on a resolved fibre product -> Hodge map between compactified third cohomologies
- **r-two-substitution** [claim](#stmt-comp-twosquares) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-models-2): Spread cycles over a dominating stratum, conic cylinders, boundary exclusion by countable Hodge conditions -> Correspondence Theta_b inducing the variation isomorphism
- **r-curve-source** [claim](#stmt-final-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-curves): Compose the surface correspondence with the curve injection -> Algebraic embedding into the tensor of curve cohomologies
- **r-half-spin** [claim](#stmt-final-spin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-spin): Lie algebra splitting, weight-one effectivity leaves half-spins, multiplicity-space spanning and rational descent -> Spanning of Hodge maps into the Clifford tensor square
- **r-generic-transfer** [claim](#stmt-final-generic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-generic): Witt extension and isometry realization, then spanning lemma -> Prescribed tensor realized at construction periods
- **r-proper-images** [claim](#stmt-final-hilbert) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-hilbert): Relative Hilbert incidence spaces, constancy of Chern character classes, proper mapping theorem -> Successful set is a countable union of closed analytic images
- **r-ambient** [claim](#stmt-specialization-ambient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-specialization-rational-density-2): Move a construction period into the ball by rational density; a seed outside all proper images forces one image to be the ball -> One fixed tensor realized on all of the perpendicular of P
- **r-compression** [claim](#stmt-transfer-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-compression): Tensor-slot bookkeeping and graphs of homomorphisms -> Algebraic compression of tensors
- **r-ample-tensor** [claim](#stmt-transfer-ample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-ample): Algebraic source projector, change of right factor, compression to the transcendental submodule -> Exact tensor on ample P-polarized surfaces
- **r-period-isometry** [claim](#stmt-transfer-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-period): Density, Witt extension and isometry realization -> Algebraic Hodge isometry to an ample P-polarized surface
- **r-tensor-transfer** [claim](#stmt-transfer-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-tensor): Transport the Clifford structure along the isometry -> Exact tensor algebraic under the embedding hypothesis
- **r-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-tensor-2): Apply the criterion to the algebraic tensor -> HC and GHC for all self-powers
- **r-moduli** [claim](#stmt-transfer-moduli) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-moduli): Motivic decomposition and transfer of coniveau -> HC and GHC for powers of Hilbert schemes and moduli spaces
