 \mathbb C\oplus\bigwedge^2U_{\mathbb C}\oplus\bigwedge^4_\pm U_{\mathbb C}.
 \end{aligned}$$ The choice of the signs on $\bigwedge^4_\pm U_\mathbb C$ follows that of $S_\pm$. To check the needed multiplicity, $\bigwedge^3U_\mathbb C$ has no standard summand. Indeed, an equivariant map from $U_\mathbb C$ to this exterior power would send a unit vector $u$ to an invariant of its stabilizer $\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(u^\perp)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}_7$. Neither $\bigwedge^3u^\perp$ nor $u\wedge\bigwedge^2u^\perp$ has such an invariant: determinant-one sign changes in an orthonormal basis kill every exterior basis coefficient. Thus the map vanishes on the orbit of $u$, which spans $U_\mathbb C$. The standard summand in each opposite-sign product has multiplicity one. There is none in a same-sign product, since $-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(U_\mathbb C)$ acts trivially on all even exterior powers and by $-1$ on $U_\mathbb C$.

Choosing a nonzero equivariant map from $U_\mathbb C$ into each opposite-sign product identifies the complex target space with $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathfrak s_\mathbb C}(U_\mathbb C,W_\mathbb C\otimes W_\mathbb C)
 \simeq (N_+\otimes N_-)\oplus(N_-\otimes N_+).$$ The ordinary flip interchanges these two summands. In particular, Equation <a href="#final:hom-equality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="final:hom-equality">[final:hom-equality]</a> and base change give $$\dim_\mathbb Q\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{HS}}(U,W\otimes W)=8^2+8^2=128.$$ It remains to generate both multiplicity spaces from the one map $f$.

A tensor involving a trivial $\mathfrak s$-constituent of either $W_i$ is a sum of trivial or half-spin representations, so it also contains no standard summand. Therefore $f_{\mathbb C}$ is described by two multiplicity tensors $$t_{+-}\in M_{1,+}\otimes M_{2,-},
 \qquad
 t_{-+}\in M_{1,-}\otimes M_{2,+},$$ at least one of which is nonzero.

Suppose, for example, that $t_{+-}\ne0$. Choose linear functionals $\ell_1,\ell_2$ with $(\ell_1\otimes\ell_2)(t_{+-})\ne0$. For arbitrary $n_+\in N_+$ and $n_-\in N_-$, the rank-one maps $m\mapsto\ell_1(m)n_+$ and $m\mapsto\ell_2(m)n_-$ send this component to a nonzero scalar multiple of $n_+\otimes n_-$. Taking the maps zero on the other signs isolates the component. Such decomposable tensors span $N_+\otimes N_-$, and the independent second copy of $\mathcal H$ in $\Phi$ supplies the other orientation by $\tau$. The case $t_{-+}\ne0$ is identical. Hence $\Phi_{\mathbb C}$ is surjective. Its rational cokernel has zero complexification and is therefore zero, proving surjectivity of $\Phi$. This descent accounts for arbitrary rational forms and Schur multiplicities; neither half-spin type need be defined separately over $\mathbb Q$.

*Algebraic realization.* Let $a:H^1(C_i,\mathbb Q)\to H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$ be a rational Hodge map. Poincaré duality on $C_i$ identifies it with a unique rational class $\alpha_a$ in the middle Künneth summand $H^1(C_i,\mathbb Q)\otimes H^1(A,\mathbb Q)\subset H^2(C_i\times A,\mathbb Q)$, normalized by $$(p_A)_*(\alpha_a\smile p_{C_i}^*x)=a(x).$$ The Hodge condition says precisely that $\alpha_a$ has type $(1,1)$. After clearing denominators, the exponential sequence realizes it as the first Chern class of a holomorphic line bundle: the obstruction is its zero image in $H^2(\mathcal O_{C_i\times A})$. Projective GAGA makes this line bundle algebraic. Dividing by the same denominator gives a divisor $D_a\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^1(C_i\times A)_\mathbb Q$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{cl}}(D_a)=\alpha_a$. Thus its cohomology class has exactly the required middle component.

For maps $a:H^1(C_1)\to W$ and $b:H^1(C_2)\to W$, the exterior product $D_a\boxtimes D_b$, with its factors ordered as $(C_1\times C_2)\times(A\times A)$, has codimension two and acts on $H^1(C_1)\otimes H^1(C_2)$ by $a\otimes b$. If $Z$ is the given cycle from a surface, its composition with this product has codimension $2+2-2=2$ and has image in $W\otimes W$.

Let $s:A\times A\to A\times A$ interchange the factors. For $u,v\in H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$, $$s_*\bigl(p_1^*u\smile p_2^*v\bigr)
 =p_2^*u\smile p_1^*v
 =-p_1^*v\smile p_2^*u.$$ Consequently $-\Gamma_s$ induces the ordinary tensor flip $\tau$ on this summand. Given a target map $h$, choose a rational preimage under $\Phi$ and write it as a finite sum $$h=\sum_\nu c_\nu(a_\nu\otimes b_\nu)f
   +\sum_\mu d_\mu\tau(a'_\mu\otimes b'_\mu)f.$$ It is realized exactly by the rational cycle $$\sum_\nu c_\nu(D_{a_\nu}\boxtimes D_{b_\nu})\circ Z
 -\sum_\mu d_\mu\Gamma_s\circ
       (D_{a'_\mu}\boxtimes D_{b'_\mu})\circ Z.$$ Composition with $\Gamma_s$, whose codimension is $\dim(A\times A)$, also preserves codimension two. On the specified source subspace the resulting action equals $h$ in the full $H^2(A\times A,\mathbb Q)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-48"></a>
#### Transfer by a rational Hodge isometry

<a id="stmt-final-generic"></a>
<div id="final:generic" class="proposition">

**Proposition 31**. *Let $b$ be a construction point of Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a>, and let $S$ be a projective K3 surface equipped with a rational Hodge isometry $r_T:T_S\xrightarrow{\sim}U_b=T_{S_b^0}$ for the cup-product forms. For every full even-Clifford model $A$ of $T_S$, every rational nonisotropic right factor $w$, and every rational polarization $E$, there is a cycle $$\Gamma_S\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A\times A)_\mathbb Q,
 \qquad (\Gamma_S)_*x=\iota_{w,E}(x)\quad(x\in T_S),$$ where the equality holds in $H^2(A\times A,\mathbb Q)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-final-generic"><summary>Proof of stmt-final-generic</summary>

*Proof.* The assumed $r_T$ uses the form on $U_b$ inherited from $Y$, which the auxiliary K3 realizes isometrically. The factor-two pullback to $X_b$ enters only the curve correspondence of Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a> and is not part of this isometry.

Choose markings of the full second cohomology spaces by $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3},\mathbb Q}$. They turn $r_T$ into an isometry between two nondegenerate rational subspaces of this one quadratic space. Witt’s Extension Theorem <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[PetersSterk](#ref-PetersSterk), Corollary 7.2.8] extends it to an isometry of $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3},\mathbb Q}$. Returning through the markings gives $$r:H^2(S,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^2(S_b^0,\mathbb Q).$$ The isometry $r$ maps the orthogonal complement of $T_S$ to the orthogonal complement of $T_{S_b^0}$. These are exactly $\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(S)_\mathbb Q$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(S_b^0)_\mathbb Q$, both of type $(1,1)$. Since its restriction $r_T$ is Hodge, $r$ is therefore a rational Hodge isometry on the full second cohomology. Buskin’s Theorem <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[Buskin](#ref-Buskin), Theorem 1.1], applied to these smooth projective complex K3 surfaces, gives a cycle $$R\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times S_b^0)_\mathbb Q,\qquad
 R_*|_{H^2(S,\mathbb Q)}=r.$$ The hypotheses here are precisely full rational Hodge isometry and projectivity; the integral Néron–Severi lattice of $S_b^0$ is not required to equal $P$.

Compose $R$ with the cycle $Z$ of Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a>. The resulting codimension-two correspondence induces the injection $$f_S:T_S\xrightarrow{r_T}U_b
       \xrightarrow{f}H^1(C_1,\mathbb Q)\otimes H^1(C_2,\mathbb Q).$$ At these periods $\mathop{\mathrm{Hg}}(T_S)=\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(T_S)$. Apply Lemma <a href="#final:spin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:spin">30</a> to $f_S$ and to the given full even-Clifford model $W=H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$ of dimension $128$. The prescribed map $\iota_{w,E}$ of Equation <a href="#final:specified-tensor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="final:specified-tensor">[final:specified-tensor]</a> belongs to the target of $\Phi$. A rational preimage under $\Phi$, realized by the divisor products and signed interchange in that lemma, supplies $\Gamma_S$ with exactly the asserted action. In particular the equality is on all of $T_S$ and has no extra component in the other Künneth summands of $H^2(A\times A,\mathbb Q)$.

The choices of abelian data, pencil, and final parameter retain the successive order of Lemma <a href="#geom:genericity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:genericity">16</a> and Corollary <a href="#comp:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:surface">28</a>. We have used the full Hodge group only at these construction periods. Proposition <a href="#specialization:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="specialization:ambient">33</a> will realize one fixed standard tensor throughout a target period ball, including Picard jumps. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 32*. Buskin’s theorem does not require integrality or preservation of an ample chamber. In its cyclic reduction, order one means an integral isometry. After a possible sign change and finitely many reflections in algebraic $(-2)$-classes, it maps a Kähler chamber to a Kähler chamber, so strong Torelli realizes it by an isomorphism; its graph and the sign and reflection correspondences are algebraic. In the final rational-reflection chain, transport an initial integral ample class through the partial isometries. On each intermediate period-realized K3 it is a rational $(1,1)$-class of positive square. Clearing denominators gives an integral such class, so the K3 projectivity criterion makes every intermediate surface projective, as required for the cyclic steps.

</div>

<a id="specialization:section"></a>
### Algebraic cycles at every period

Proposition <a href="#final:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:generic">31</a> constructs the prescribed correspondence at the selected construction periods of Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a>. To reach a Picard-jump target, we keep the perpendicular of $P$ fixed, rather than varying the source with the transcendental space.

<a id="stmt-specialization-ambient"></a>
<div id="specialization:ambient" class="proposition">

**Proposition 33**. *Let $S$ have an ample primitive $P$-polarization and put $U_P=P_{\mathbb Q}^{\perp}\subset H^2(S,\mathbb Q)$. There are a full even-Clifford realization $W_U=C^+(U_P,q)=H^1(A_U,\mathbb Q)$, a rational polarization $E_0$, a nonisotropic rational $w_0\in U_P$, and a cycle $$\Gamma_U\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(S\times A_U^2)_{\mathbb Q}$$ whose full degree-two action on every $x\in U_P$ is exactly $\iota_{w_0,E_0}(x)$. This assertion concerns the whole fixed rank-eight space, including its rational $(1,1)$ classes at a Picard jump.*

</div>

The parameter-space lemma below records equality of a cycle’s action on all of this fixed source. Its proper images will supply actual cycles at every target, without specializing the curves used to construct a seed.

<a id="pm-h-50"></a>
#### Proper parameter spaces of successful cycles

<a id="stmt-final-hilbert"></a>
<div id="final:hilbert" class="lemma">

<a id="final:cycle-condition"></a>
**Lemma 34**. *Let $f:\mathcal Z\to B$ be a smooth proper holomorphic family of pure relative dimension $n$ over a connected coordinate ball, with a closed relative embedding $\mathcal Z\subset\mathbf P^N\times B$. Fix an integer $0\leq r\leq n$, a rational local system $\mathcal V$ on $B$, a morphism of local systems $$L:R^{2r}f_*\mathbb Q\longrightarrow\mathcal V,$$ and a flat section $\tau$ of $\mathcal V$. The set of $b\in B$ admitting a rational codimension-$r$ algebraic cycle $Z_b$ such that $$\label{final:cycle-condition}
 L_b\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{cl}}(Z_b)\bigr)=\tau_b$$ is a countable union of closed analytic subsets of $B$. Each subset in this union is the image of a proper analytic parameter space carrying a fixed rational combination of universal flat subschemes; every point of that space supplies a cycle satisfying <a href="#final:cycle-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="final:cycle-condition">[final:cycle-condition]</a>.*

*If the successful set contains a nonempty open subset of $B$, it equals $B$. The same conclusion holds if, for every countable union of proper closed analytic subsets of $B$, there is a successful point outside that union.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-final-hilbert"><summary>Proof of stmt-final-hilbert</summary>

*Proof.* *Embedded parameters over an analytic base.* Let $R$ be the Hilbert polynomial of the embedded fibres of $f$; it is constant because the family is flat. For any fixed Hilbert polynomial $Q$, write $K_Q=\operatorname{Hilb}^{Q}(\mathbf P^N)$. This is a projective scheme with a flat universal subscheme, by the projective Hilbert-scheme theorem <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[GrothendieckHilbert](#ref-GrothendieckHilbert), Theorem 3.2]. We use the incidence space $$H_Q=\{(b,z)\in B\times K_Q^{\mathrm{an}}:
                    \mathcal U_z\subset\mathcal Z_b\}.$$ It is closed analytic, with its natural incidence structure. To see this directly, let $\mathcal J$ be the ideal sheaf of $\mathcal Z$ in $\mathbf P^N\times B$. The bounded regularity for the fixed polynomials $R$ and $Q$ supplies an integer $k$ such that $\mathcal J(k)$ is relatively generated, its direct image is locally free with base change, and the universal subscheme for $Q$ has the corresponding vanishing and base-change properties. On $B\times K_Q^{\mathrm{an}}$, restriction defines a holomorphic vector-bundle map from sections of $\mathcal J(k)$ to sections of $\mathcal O_{\mathcal U}(k)$. Its zero scheme is exactly $H_Q$: vanishing of these generating sections is equivalent to containment of subschemes. Since $K_Q^{\mathrm{an}}$ is compact, $H_Q\to B$ is proper. Its universal subscheme is flat over $H_Q$, being the pullback of the universal Hilbert family. No algebraicity assumption on $B$ or on $f$ is required.

*The ambient Betti class on singular parameters.* For polynomials of degree $n-r$, associate to each universal fibre its $(n-r)$-dimensional fundamental cycle, with the generic lengths of its structure sheaf as multiplicities. We claim that its class in the ambient $H^{2r}(\mathcal Z_b,\mathbb Q)$ is locally constant on $H_Q$, including at singular or nonreduced Hilbert parameters.

Here are details that justify this assertion in the analytic setting. The same high-degree ideal construction gives a holomorphic classifying map $B\to\operatorname{Hilb}^{R}(\mathbf P^N)^{\mathrm{an}}$: the bundle of degree-$k$ ideals defines the map to the Hilbert scheme’s Grassmannian realization. Its image is in the open locus $K_R^{\mathrm{sm}}$ of smooth, pure $n$-dimensional fibres. Over this algebraic locus let $\mathfrak Z\to K_R^{\mathrm{sm}}$ be the universal smooth family, and let $\mathfrak H_Q$ be its relative Hilbert scheme. Thus $H_Q$ and its universal pair are analytic pullbacks of this algebraic universal pair.

On $\mathfrak Z\times_{K_R^{\mathrm{sm}}}\mathfrak H_Q$, the universal structure sheaf $\mathcal F$ is flat over $\mathfrak H_Q$ and is a perfect complex on the ambient space. For this last assertion, take successive locally free surjections onto $\mathcal F$ and their kernels on the quasi-projective ambient space. All the kernels remain flat over $\mathfrak H_Q$. The fibres of the ambient family are regular of dimension $n$, so after at most $n$ syzygies the fibrewise kernels are locally free. The local flatness criterion then makes this kernel locally free on the ambient space. This gives a bounded vector-bundle resolution even when $\mathfrak H_Q$ is singular. Flatness over the parameter space also shows that the resolution restricts to a resolution of the ordinary structure sheaf on every ambient fibre.

<a id="specialization:leading-character"></a>
The topological Chern character of this complex, pulled back to the analytic universal pair, consequently satisfies $$\label{specialization:leading-character}
 \operatorname{ch}_r(\mathcal F)|_{\mathcal Z_b}
       =\mathop{\mathrm{cl}}\bigl([\mathcal U_z]_{n-r}\bigr)
       \quad\text{in }H^{2r}(\mathcal Z_b,\mathbb Q).$$ This is the leading Chern-character identity for a coherent sheaf supported in codimension at least $r$. It follows from Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch (GRR) and dévissage, followed by the comparison of algebraic and topological Chern classes <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[Fulton](#ref-Fulton), Theorem 15.2, Example 15.2.16(a), and Proposition 19.1.2]. For the precise form needed here, remove a subset of codimension at least $r+1$: dévissage then reduces to the generic lengths on smooth codimension-$r$ supports, where the regular-embedding case of GRR gives their fundamental classes as the leading terms. The removed subset contributes nothing in degree $2r$, since its cohomology with supports vanishes below degree $2r+2$ <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[Fulton](#ref-Fulton), Section 19.1, equation (3) and Lemma 19.1.1]. This also explains why lower-dimensional embedded components cause no problem.

A class on the total space of a pulled-back smooth proper family restricts to a flat section of its fibre cohomology. Indeed, locally trivialize the original differentiable family over $B$ and pull that trivialization back to the parameter space; inclusions of fibres along a path are homotopic. Complex analytic spaces are locally path connected, and their nilpotents do not change their topology. Equation <a href="#specialization:leading-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="specialization:leading-character">[specialization:leading-character]</a> proves the claimed constancy on all connected components, without assuming that the universal subschemes themselves are smooth.

*Successful components and their images.* Take every finite list $Q_1,\ldots,Q_s$ of polynomials of degree $n-r$ and every list $c_1,\ldots,c_s\in\mathbb Q$, and form $$H_{\boldsymbol Q}=H_{Q_1}\times_B\cdots\times_B H_{Q_s},
 \qquad \mathcal Z_{\boldsymbol c,z}
       =\sum_{i=1}^s c_i[\mathcal U_{i,z}]_{n-r}.$$ The empty list represents the zero cycle. These spaces are proper over $B$, and the displayed universal combinations have constant ambient Betti class on each connected component. Because $B$ is simply connected, both $L$ and $\tau$ are constant in local-system trivializations. Hence <a href="#final:cycle-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="final:cycle-condition">[final:cycle-condition]</a> holds either everywhere or nowhere on each component.

Retain exactly the successful components. There are countably many polynomial and coefficient lists and countably many components: all the parameter spaces are second countable and locally connected, so their connected components are open and closed. Each retained component is a closed analytic subspace, proper over $B$. Its image is closed analytic by Remmert’s proper mapping theorem; for a primary statement, see <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[GrauertProperImage](#ref-GrauertProperImage), 7.1, Satz 1], applied to the reduction of the component. Reduction changes neither its points nor its image. For a textbook statement of the proper mapping theorem, see <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[Demailly2012](#ref-Demailly2012), Chapter II, Section 8.2, Theorem 8.8].

Every rational algebraic cycle on a fibre is a finite rational sum of integral subvarieties and therefore occurs in this list. Conversely, every point of any retained image has an actual universal fibre combination above it. Their supports in the projective fibre are algebraic by Chow’s theorem, so their fundamental cycles are algebraic. We have proved that the successful set is exactly the stated countable union, with the asserted cycle-theoretic meaning at every image point.

A proper closed analytic subset of a connected ball is nowhere dense. If the union contains a nonempty open set, Baire’s theorem forces one image to have interior; the analytic identity principle makes that image all of $B$. If instead the union escapes every countable union of proper closed analytic subsets, the same conclusion follows: otherwise the successful images would themselves be such a union. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-51"></a>
#### Realization in an ample period ball

We now apply the proper-parameter lemma directly in a small period ball through the desired target. A rational change of period coordinates places the generic geometric construction in that ball; it does not change the algebraic pencil used in the construction.

<a id="stmt-specialization-rational-density"></a>
<div id="specialization:rational-density" class="lemma">

**Lemma 35**. *Let $(V,q)$ be a rational quadratic space of signature $(2,n)$. The group $O(V)(\mathbb Q)$ is dense in $O(V_{\mathbb R})$, and the rational orbit of every point of its type-IV period domain meets every nonempty open subset of that domain.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-specialization-rational-density"><summary>Proof of stmt-specialization-rational-density</summary>

*Proof.* Cartan–Dieudonné expresses each real orthogonal transformation as a finite product of reflections in nonisotropic real vectors. The reflection $$r_v(x)=x-\frac{2q(x,v)}{q(v,v)}v$$ depends continuously on $v$ on the nonisotropic open and is rational for rational $v$. Approximating the finitely many vectors proves density. The full real orthogonal group acts transitively on the oriented positive two-planes, which are the period-domain points; it also interchanges the two components. Density proves the orbit assertion. We use the standard reflection theorem in <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[PetersSterk](#ref-PetersSterk), Theorem 7.2.4]. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-specialization-rational-density-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-specialization-rational-density</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#specialization:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="specialization:ambient">33</a>.* *The fixed local family and tensor.* Choose a marking of the target and an integral ample class $h\in P$. The local K3 period theorem gives a six-dimensional marked deformation ball $B$ through $S$ on which $P$ remains algebraic, identified with an open subset of the period domain of $U_P$. The class $h$ lifts locally to a line bundle; shrink $B$ so it is relatively ample and a power embeds the family $\mathcal S\to B$ projectively. Only $P$, not the target’s whole Picard group, is kept algebraic. Thus $U_P$ is a fixed nondegenerate rational Hodge subvariation even at jumps. These local assertions follow from local Torelli and the deformation theory of polarized K3 surfaces <a id="pm-cite-65"></a>[[Huybrechts](#ref-Huybrechts)].

Fix any rational $w_0\in U_P$ with $q(w_0,w_0)\ne0$ and put $W_U=C^+(U_P,q)$. There is one rational alternating form polarizing the left Clifford Hodge structures on the chosen period component. Here is a construction that also fixes its characters. Let $x^*$ be reversal and $B_C(x,y)=\operatorname{scal}(x^*y)$. Choose two orthogonal positive rational vectors with oriented product $\beta$ in that component and set, up to an overall sign, $$E_0(x,y)=B_C(x,y\beta).$$ Reversal sends $\beta$ to $-\beta$, and cyclic invariance of scalar part proves alternation. Left spin multiplication preserves $E_0$ because $g^*g=1$. At the chosen plane write $\beta=cJ$, $c>0$. For an even orthogonal monomial containing $a$ of the two positive generators, $B_C(x,x)$ has sign $(-1)^a$ and $Jx\beta=(-1)^{a+1}cx$. Thus $E_0(x,Jx)$ is definite after one overall choice of sign. Transitivity of the connected real spin group on the component proves positivity throughout it. This is the standard Kuga–Satake polarization <a id="pm-cite-66"></a>[[KS](#ref-KS)].

The $(1,0)$ space of $W_U$ is $\ker L_\sigma$ for a period vector $\sigma$, and varies holomorphically. A rational symplectic basis for $E_0$ and its self-dual integral lattice give, over a smaller $B$, a polarized holomorphic abelian family $\mathcal A\to B$ with this fixed rational $H^1$. The usual torus construction over Siegel space and a relative theta bundle give a relative projective embedding after shrinking. The tensor $\tau=\iota_{w_0,E_0}$ is a fixed rational linear map $U_P\to W_U\otimes W_U$ and a Hodge map at every period, by spin equivariance of $j_{w_0}$.

*A geometric period chart in the target ball.* Choose a geometric datum in the simultaneous regular-period and domination open of Sections <a href="#geom:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:section">3</a> and <a href="#domination:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="domination:section">4</a>. On a local marking fix a rational isometry $\theta:U\simeq U_P$ for the form inherited from the quotient surface $Y$; this is possible by Proposition <a href="#geom:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:structure">9</a> and Lemma <a href="#main:lattice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:lattice">3</a>. Lemma <a href="#specialization:rational-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="specialization:rational-density">35</a> gives a rational $g\in O(U_P)(\mathbb Q)$ moving its period into $B$. Fix $g$ and $\theta$, and shrink the original normalized geometric product chart so that its period map followed by $g\theta$ is a submersion into $B$. The change $g\theta$ acts only on rational period coordinates. Pencils subsequently selected still have the form $d_0+td_1$ in the normalized algebraic section chart, with directions in the original good open at infinity.

*Proper images for the full action.* Apply Lemma <a href="#final:hilbert" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:hilbert">34</a> to $\mathcal Z=\mathcal S\times_B\mathcal A^2$, of relative dimension $130$, with $r=2$ and $$\mathcal V=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb Q}\bigl(U_P,R^2(\mathcal A^2/B)_*\mathbb Q\bigr),
 \qquad
 L_b(\gamma)(x)=(p_{AA})_*(\gamma\smile p_S^*x).$$ The success condition is equality to $\tau$ on all of $U_P$ in the full target $H^2(A_b^2)$, not only after a projection or on the holomorphic line. It is a flat local-system condition. The successful set is therefore a countable union of closed analytic images $Y_i$ of proper cycle-parameter spaces, with actual successful cycles over every point of each image.

*A seed outside every proposed proper image.* Suppose every $Y_i$ is proper. In the regular construction period chart, pull back the $Y_i$ by the fixed map $g\theta$ and also pull back the extra-Picard hyperplanes in $B$. These are countably many proper closed analytic loci: the period submersion is open, and an extra integral class has a nonzero $U_P$ projection because $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}\cap P_{\mathbb Q}=P$. Its period equation is therefore nontrivial. Include also the proper marked Hodge-tensor conditions used in Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a>.

Apply Lemma <a href="#geom:genericity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:genericity">16</a> to this prescribed list, using the admissible pencil open of the construction. It selects abelian data, then an actual admissible section pencil with good leading section. Once that pencil, its first models, and its relative correspondence are fixed, intersect its algebraic good open with that of Corollary <a href="#comp:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:surface">28</a> and add the corollary’s countable exceptional parameters. The intersection is still a nonempty algebraic open of the pencil. The last choice in the lemma supplies a point in this open outside those exceptions and every prescribed period locus. In particular, the full special orthogonal Hodge group and the curve source of Lemma <a href="#final:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:curves">29</a> hold at this construction point.

The target fiber at its moved period has $\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}=P$, so its full $U_P$ is transcendental. The auxiliary K3 has transcendental space $U$. The rational period isometry $g\theta$ identifies these transcendental Hodge structures with their cup forms. Proposition <a href="#final:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:generic">31</a> therefore supplies on the target fiber a cycle realizing exactly the fixed tensor $\tau$. That period lies in a successful image, contrary to its choice. Thus some image is all of $B$, and its fiber over the original target supplies $\Gamma_U$.

The rank-eight source and its full action have remained fixed throughout the ball. Neither a global moduli cycle nor specialization of a smooth curve presentation is required. ◻

</details>

<a id="transfer:section"></a>
### Exact restriction and rational transfer

We first convert the fixed-source result to the standard tensor of the actual transcendental space at an ample $P$-polarized surface. A separate rational period transfer will then reach every surface in the quadratic locus of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-53"></a>
#### Restriction at Picard jumps

At a Picard jump the inclusion $T_S\subset U_P$ induces an inclusion of full even-Clifford modules. The following identity compresses the ambient tensor to this submodule with its prescribed polarization; it does not require the ambient polarization to restrict to that choice.

<a id="stmt-transfer-compression"></a>
<div id="transfer:compression" class="lemma">

<a id="transfer:compression-identity"></a>
**Lemma 36**. *Let $i:W_T\hookrightarrow W_U$ be an inclusion of polarizable rational weight-one Hodge structures, let $p:W_U\to W_T$ be a Hodge retraction, and let $E_U,E_T$ be rational polarizations. Let $H$ be a rational Hodge structure of weight zero. For a Hodge map $j:H\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(W_U)$, write $\iota_{j,E_U}:H(-1)\to W_U\otimes W_U$ for its tensor form. Set $$b=(E_T^\flat)^{-1}i^\vee(-1)E_U^\flat:W_U\longrightarrow W_T.$$ Then $$\label{transfer:compression-identity}
 (p\otimes b)\iota_{j,E_U}=\iota_{pji,E_T}.$$ If the weight-one structures are abelian $H^1$ spaces, both maps on the tensor slots are induced by rational algebraic correspondences.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-transfer-compression"><summary>Proof of stmt-transfer-compression</summary>

*Proof.* In the ordering $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(W_U)=W_U\otimes W_U^\vee$, tensor conversion is $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\otimes(E_U^\flat)^{-1}$ after the common Tate twist. Hence the left side of <a href="#transfer:compression-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="transfer:compression-identity">[transfer:compression-identity]</a> is $$\bigl(p\otimes(E_T^\flat)^{-1}i^\vee(-1)\bigr)j,$$ which is exactly the tensor conversion of $pji$. All maps are rational Hodge maps. For completeness, if $f:H^1(B,\mathbb Q)\to H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$ is any such map, its dual on homology commutes with complex structures. For some positive integer $n$, $nf^\vee$ is integral and induces a holomorphic homomorphism $\phi:A\to B$. Projectivity and GAGA make $\phi$ algebraic, and $f=\phi^*/n$. Thus $[\Gamma_\phi^t]/n\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^{\dim B}(B\times A)_{\mathbb Q}$ induces $f$ on $H^1$. No equality of dimensions or isogeny assumption is needed. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-transfer-ample"></a>
<div id="transfer:ample" class="proposition">

**Proposition 37**. *Let $S$ be an ample $P$-polarized K3 surface. For every prescribed nonisotropic $w\in T_S$, rational polarization $E$ and transported even-Clifford realization of $C^+(T_S,q)$, its standard tensor $\iota_{w,E}$ has a rational codimension-two algebraic realization.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-transfer-ample"><summary>Proof of stmt-transfer-ample</summary>

*Proof.* Write $U=U_P$ and $M=U\cap\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(S)_{\mathbb Q}$. Since $P$ contains an ample class, Hodge index makes $M$ negative definite and $$U=T_S\perp M.$$ The source projection to $T_S$ is algebraic: choose divisor bases for $\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(S)_{\mathbb Q}$ and use their inverse intersection matrix together with the diagonal. Proposition <a href="#specialization:ambient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="specialization:ambient">33</a> gives one exact tensor $\iota_{w_0,E_0}$ on all of $U$.

For the desired $w\in T_S$, put $r=w_0^{-1}w\in C^+(U,q)$. It is invertible. Right multiplication $R_r$ commutes with the left Hodge complex structure, so is a rational Hodge automorphism, and associativity gives $$j_w=R_rj_{w_0},\qquad
 \iota_{w,E_0}=(R_r\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}})\iota_{w_0,E_0}.$$ This is a change of the first, output tensor slot.

The natural Clifford inclusion $i:W_T=C^+(T_S,q)\hookrightarrow W_U=C^+(U,q)$ is injective by PBW and is Hodge: the left period bivector lies in $C^+(T_S,q)$ on both spaces. Polarizability gives a rational Hodge retraction $p$. Apply Lemma <a href="#transfer:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="transfer:compression">36</a> with the desired $E$ on $W_T$. For $v,w\in T_S$, $j_w(v)$ preserves $W_T$ and restricts there to the standard operator $a\mapsto vaw$. Therefore $$\bigl(pR_r\otimes(E^\flat)^{-1}i^\vee(-1)E_0^\flat\bigr)
       \iota_{w_0,E_0}
 =\iota^{T_S}_{w,E}.$$ The order matters: $R_r$ itself need not preserve $W_T$ when $w_0\notin T_S$, but the resulting operator $j_w$ does.

Both tensor-slot maps are algebraic on abelian $H^1$ by the lemma. After clearing denominators, the single product homomorphism on the two abelian factors and its normalized transpose graph realize their tensor product, with no unrecorded permutation sign. The normalized transpose graph has codimension $2\dim A_U=\dim A_U^2$, so postcomposition preserves codimension two. Precompose with the algebraic source projector and postcompose with algebraic degree-one projectors on the abelian factors. The full degree-two action is then precisely the displayed tensor. The same argument with the prescribed rational Hodge identification transports it to any stipulated isogenous model. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-54"></a>
#### Transfer to the rational quadratic locus

<a id="stmt-transfer-period"></a>
<div id="transfer:period" class="lemma">

**Lemma 38**. *Let $S$ be a projective K3 surface with a rational isometric embedding $\iota:T_S\hookrightarrow V_P$. There is an ample $P$-polarized K3 surface $R$ and a full rational Hodge isometry $$\Phi:H^2(S,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^2(R,\mathbb Q),\qquad
 \Phi(T_S)=T_R.$$ Both directions of this isometry are induced by algebraic correspondences.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-transfer-period"><summary>Proof of stmt-transfer-period</summary>

*Proof.* Use the primitive embedding in Lemma <a href="#main:lattice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:lattice">3</a> and an exact-$P$ surface supplied there. Choose an integral ample $h\in P$ and a small marked $P$-polarized period patch $\Omega$ about that surface, shrinking so that $h$ stays ample. This is an actual open image in the period domain of $V_P$, and includes its Picard-jump fibers.

The line $z=\iota(H^{2,0}(S))$ is a point of this period domain. Lemma <a href="#specialization:rational-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="specialization:rational-density">35</a> gives $g\in O(V_P)(\mathbb Q)$ with $gz\in\Omega$. Choose the fiber $R$ with period exactly $gz$. Then $g\iota:T_S\to H^2(R,\mathbb Q)$ is an isometry taking the holomorphic line to that of $R$. The two full second-cohomology spaces have the rational K3 form. Witt’s extension theorem extends this map to a full rational isometry $\Phi$. It preserves the holomorphic line, its conjugate by rationality, and their orthogonal complement, which is the $(1,1)$ space. Hence $\Phi$ is Hodge. Lefschetz $(1,1)$ gives $\Phi(\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(S)_{\mathbb Q})=\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(R)_{\mathbb Q}$ and therefore $\Phi(T_S)=T_R$. Buskin’s theorem <a id="pm-cite-67"></a>[[Buskin](#ref-Buskin), Theorem 1.1] algebraizes $\Phi$ and its inverse. It requires neither integrality nor preservation of an ample class. Density was used only to enter the patch; the comparison uses the exact realized period. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-transfer-tensor"></a>
<div id="transfer:tensor" class="proposition">

**Proposition 39**. *Under the isometric-embedding hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">2</a>, the exact tensor $\iota_{w,E}$ on $T_S$ is algebraic for every stipulated $w$, $E$, and transported isogenous even-Clifford model.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-transfer-tensor"><summary>Proof of stmt-transfer-tensor</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $R,\Phi$ by Lemma <a href="#transfer:period" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="transfer:period">38</a> and set $\phi=\Phi|_{T_S}:T_S\simeq T_R$. The isometry induces a unital Clifford algebra isomorphism $$c_\phi:C^+(T_S,q)\xrightarrow{\sim}C^+(T_R,q),
 \qquad c_\phi(vaw)=\phi(v)c_\phi(a)\phi(w).$$ It is a weight-one Hodge isomorphism because it takes the oriented period bivector defining left multiplication to the corresponding bivector for $R$. For the broader functoriality under Hodge similarities, see <a id="pm-cite-68"></a>[[VarescoSimilarities](#ref-VarescoSimilarities), Proposition 3.1 and Lemma 3.5]; the multiplier here is one, with the prescribed right factor transported as in the displayed identity. Proposition <a href="#transfer:ample" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="transfer:ample">37</a> gives the standard tensor on $T_R$ for $\phi(w)$ and the polarization transported from $E$. Precompose its cycle with the algebraic correspondence for $\Phi$ and transport both abelian slots by $c_\phi^{-1}$. These rational Hodge maps on $H^1$ are algebraic by Lemma <a href="#transfer:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="transfer:compression">36</a>. The displayed Clifford identity proves equality with the specified tensor, not merely with an abstract Hodge embedding. Source and degree-one target projectors isolate its full action. All operations are degree-preserving on their intermediate factors, so the resulting cycle has codimension two. The same algebraic transport treats the stipulated isogenous model. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-transfer-tensor-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-transfer-tensor</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">2</a>.* Proposition <a href="#transfer:tensor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="transfer:tensor">39</a> proves the exact standard-tensor assertion under the stated rational isometric-embedding hypothesis, with every prescribed right vector, polarization, and transported model. Choose any one such tensor and apply Theorem <a href="#ext:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:criterion">1</a>. This gives rational HC and GHC in every degree of every self-power. For an ample $P$-polarized surface, the actual transcendental space is a nondegenerate subspace of its fixed perpendicular $U_P\simeq V_P$ by Lemma <a href="#main:lattice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:lattice">3</a>, so this case is included as asserted. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-55"></a>
#### Hilbert schemes and moduli spaces

<a id="stmt-transfer-moduli"></a>
<div id="transfer:moduli" class="corollary">

**Corollary 40**. *Let $S$ be a smooth projective complex K3 surface admitting a rational isometric embedding $(T_S,q)\hookrightarrow V_P$. For every $n,r\ge1$, rational HC and rational GHC hold in every degree of $(S^{[n]})^r$, where $S^{[n]}$ is the Hilbert scheme of length-$n$ subschemes of $S$.*

*More generally, let $\alpha\in\operatorname{Br}(S)$ and let $M$ be either a smooth projective moduli space of Gieseker-stable $\alpha$-twisted sheaves on $S$, or a smooth projective moduli space of $\sigma$-stable objects in $D^b(S,\alpha)$ for a generic Bridgeland stability condition $\sigma$. Then rational HC and rational GHC hold in every degree of $M^r$ for every $r\ge1$. If $M$ is hyperkähler, the same conclusions hold for every smooth projective hyperkähler variety birational to $M$ and all its self-powers.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-transfer-moduli"><summary>Proof of stmt-transfer-moduli</summary>

*Proof.* The assertion for zero-dimensional $M$ is immediate. Write $h(-)$ for the Chow motive with rational coefficients. The decomposition of de Cataldo–Migliorini <a id="pm-cite-69"></a>[[deCataldoMigliorini](#ref-deCataldoMigliorini), Theorem 6.2.1] expresses $h(S^{[n]})$ as a sum of Tate twists of summands of motives of powers of $S$; the summands corresponding to symmetric products are cut out by rational averaging projectors. For either stated moduli space, Bülles <a id="pm-cite-70"></a>[[BullesModuli](#ref-BullesModuli), Theorem 0.1] likewise makes $h(M)$ a direct summand of a finite sum of Tate twists of motives $h(S^k)$. Tensoring these split correspondences gives analogous splittings for every self-power, again through Tate twists of powers of $S$. Thus these varieties are motivated by $S$ in the sense of Arapura. Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">2</a> supplies HC and GHC for every power of $S$, so Arapura’s transfer lemma <a id="pm-cite-71"></a>[[ArapuraMotivation](#ref-ArapuraMotivation), Lemma 4.2], which retains geometric coniveau with its Tate shifts, gives both conjectures for every power of each indicated variety. Finally, Rieß’s specialization correspondence for birational smooth projective hyperkähler varieties <a id="pm-cite-72"></a>[[RiessBirational](#ref-RiessBirational), Theorem 3.2 and Section 3.3] gives an isomorphism of Chow motives, as formulated in <a id="pm-cite-73"></a>[[BullesModuli](#ref-BullesModuli), Proposition 1.4]. This proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>
